See [[_Sources/YT_jAdRyL6KFxs_7komnat_novaya_borovaya_52m2|extraction note]] and [[11_Budget_and_Planning/case_studies/7komnat_novaya_borovaya_52m2_case|full case study]].
- `yt_DsdLa87Acz4` — Prolife Invest (Aristarkh Babiy) Moscow flipping-renovation cost rationale (YouTube, **manually-created captions, not ASR** — lower transcription-error risk; published 2026-07-29 [metadata-confirmed]; region = Moscow, confirmed at both spoken and metadata levels). Investor/flipping perspective, not homeowner/designer — recommendations explicitly segment-scoped (comfort/business/premium/elite) and tied to resale value, not personal preference. Secondary (Moscow/RUB, not Minsk) reference, but contributes several region-agnostic durable heuristics (independent technical supervision, stretch-ceiling flood-risk reasoning, a cheaper alternative to concealed baseboards, door-height cost economics) that nuance or extend facts already in this store. See [[_Sources/YT_DsdLa87Acz4_prolife_invest_moscow_flipping|extraction note]].
- `yt_H61xa8n2nTk` — FLATART (Minsk-associated design/reno company) air-conditioning design & installation guide + retailer interview (YouTube, ASR auto-captions; published **2017-11-26 — by far the oldest source in this store**; region Minsk-associated at level 2 only (title/tags), not spoken in the transcript itself; currency ambiguous, "рублей" plausibly BYN by inference from company location, not confirmed). All pricing treated as historical-only given the 2017 date. Contributes the store's **first in-depth AC/climate-system technical content**: 3 indoor-unit placement rules, a dry-trap condensate-drainage mechanism explanation, an inverter-vs-standard explanation, a rough sizing rule of thumb (~1 kW/10 m², +20% buffer), and a top-3 buyer/installer mistakes list. Several numbers in this source are ASR-garbled or internally inconsistent (see extraction note) and were not guessed at. See [[_Sources/YT_H61xa8n2nTk_flatart_ac_installation_guide|extraction note]].
- `yt__hYkf4UkCqw` — "Дома Минска" documented renovation series, Episode 1 (YouTube; published 2025-12-11 [metadata-confirmed]; region Minsk/Belarus confirmed at level 1 — **spoken directly and repeatedly, the strongest regional evidence of any source in this store**). Episode 1 of an ongoing series following one owner's bare-shell 49 m² apartment (ЖК "Северный берег") from start to finish — **contains no completed renovation budget**, only a property purchase price (79,000 EUR / "$1,860/m²" — two different currencies stated for the same purchase with no conversion basis given, not reconciled) and undecided/leaning design choices. Contributes genuinely new **Minsk-specific replanning/approval regulatory detail** (исполком approval for wet-zone relocation, plumbing riser capping when merging bathrooms, a height threshold for wall-hung fixture approval). No case study created — no cost data exists yet to build one around; flagged as a series worth revisiting if later episodes are processed. See [[_Sources/YT__hYkf4UkCqw_doma_minska_severny_bereg_ep1|extraction note]].
- `yt_QHl1YEHMfgE` — "Дома Минска" series, Episode 2 (YouTube; published 2026-04-07 [metadata-confirmed]; same project/apartment as `yt__hYkf4UkCqw`, but region **not independently re-confirmed at level 1 in this episode's own speech** — inherited from series continuity only). A professional designer (Elena Belitskaya) walks through layout/planning principles against the real floor plan — **no pricing content at all**. Contributes the store's richest set of universal, brand-agnostic technical/planning facts to date (room-count-vs-window-count, drainage-slope-aware kitchen relocation, bed-clearance ergonomics, door-leaf-width standards, toilet-first bathroom sequencing, and a critical "zashivka vs. venshakhta" distinction — the latter genuinely important and not present in any prior source). Also contains a promotional element (the host's direct personal endorsement of the designer) flagged separately, per this store's advertising filter, without discrediting her technical claims. See [[_Sources/YT_QHl1YEHMfgE_doma_minska_severny_bereg_ep2_layout|extraction note]].
- `yt_pC7CO-rpwBs` — "Команда Мастеров" turnkey studio pitch/cost-breakdown video (YouTube; published 2024-10-01, second-oldest source in this store; region = Belarus confirmed at level 1 but **country-level only, not city-level**; Minsk association is level 2 only via Instagram handle). **Turnkey/Full-Service** by design — a 7-stage $/m² budget framework (design, labor, rough materials, finish materials, appliances, fixtures, custom furniture). **Arithmetic cross-check performed (level 4)**: the stated overall range "$1,100 to $18,800/m²" — the low end matches summing the 7 stages' own minimums almost exactly ($1,099), but the high end does not match summing the maximums ($1,749) at all; "$18,800" is treated as an ASR/speech error and **not used** — the arithmetic-reconstructed $1,749 is recorded instead, with reasoning shown. A third Belarus turnkey data point, roughly consistent with (if somewhat above) 7komnat.by's "~$1,000/m² typical" market claim. See [[_Sources/YT_pC7CO-rpwBs_komanda_masterov_7stage_budget|extraction note]].
- `yt_cdNwbqsLUK4` — "РемонтХочу" (remonthochu.ru) smeta-literacy/self-calculation guide (YouTube; published 2024-11-14; region = Moscow **and Moscow Oblast**, named directly and specifically in speech — more precise than most Moscow sources in this store). **Turnkey company's own construction-only smeta** — 11 stages, rough+finish labor (materials likely bundled per line item, `inferred` not confirmed), explicitly **excludes design fee and furniture/appliances** (unlike this store's broader turnkey totals) — do not compare its $/m² directly against those without adjusting scope. **Two arithmetic cross-checks performed (level 4)**: (1) an ASR-garbled per-m² plaster rate ("от полу тысяч") resolved to exactly 1,500 RUB/m² by dividing the stage's own stated total by its own stated area; (2) a missing flooring subtotal (never stated aloud) recovered exactly (127,500 RUB = 85 m² × 1,500 RUB/m²) by reconciling the grand total against the sum of all other explicitly-stated stages — a clean, non-guessed recovery. Contributes the store's richest set of **smeta-literacy/anti-upsell heuristics** (price by fixture points not pipe length, price electrical by floor area not cable length, demand a fully-itemized putty/skim-coat scope, verify flooring pattern is specified, watch for common-area covering requirements and mid-project scope additions) — durable and delivery-model-agnostic, a strong match for this project's stated preference for universal knowledge. See [[_Sources/YT_cdNwbqsLUK4_remonthochu_smeta_methodology|extraction note]].
- `yt_OrKB6uSKRyk` — "Бородатый Прораб" (Vladimir Vorobyov) Moscow business-class renovation cost rationale (YouTube; published 2025-01-08; region = Moscow, confirmed at level 1, city named directly and repeatedly). **Turnkey/Full-Service**, self-promotional own-company channel — the **broadest single figure in this store to date**: "from 150,000 RUB/m²" for a full, move-in-ready realization (design + labor + rough/finish materials + furniture + specialized installation). Three-tier design-project pricing structure (4,000/5,000/7,000 RUB/m²) is genuinely useful methodology. Two ASR issues: a large-format-tile rate rendered as "88,000 RUB/m²" is almost certainly a digit-duplication error for ~8,000 (contextual "×2" cue), recorded as `uncertain`; a rough-materials base rate has a **missing leading digit** in the transcript and was **not reconstructed at all** (left as a genuine gap). Contributes a nuance to the guide's existing flat "labor ≈30-35% of total" assumption (this source's own labor share drops to ~20% at premium tiers) and corroborates, from an independent practitioner, `yt_DsdLa87Acz4`'s theme that personalized high-end renovations don't resell at cost. See [[_Sources/YT_OrKB6uSKRyk_borodaty_prorab_moscow_business_class|extraction note]].
- `yt_8zm_5NjbGNA` — Яна Врублевская personal renovation vlog, "Ремонт за 10000$. Цены на все. Минск Мир" (YouTube; published 2023-05-01 [metadata-confirmed], **second-oldest source in this store after FLATART's 2017**; region "Минск Мир" named in title/tags/location-metadata, but **zero spoken mentions anywhere in the transcript** — level 2 only, checked directly). **Personal channel, not a company/contractor channel** — the apartment owner narrating her own completed self-managed studio renovation, a different promotional profile than most sources here. **Self-Managed/Itemized, the fullest end-to-end self-managed real project in this store** — covers both rough works (labor+materials, BYN) and the full finish/fixture/appliance/furniture shopping list, unlike the store's previous closest comparable (labor-only 44 m² смета). Notably transparent about exchange-rate drift affecting her own project (BYN/USD moved 2.6→2.8 during the renovation). **Level-4 arithmetic cross-check performed**: itemized rough-works line items sum to 10,665 BYN against her stated 11,250 BYN total (585 BYN gap, plausibly an unenumerated item per her own caveat, not resolved). Real-world outcome: **~30% budget overshoot** against her own stated $10,000 target (final ≈$12,957, `arithmetic-exact`). Contributes several soft-cost line items (new-build handover inspection, temporary toilet on day one, per-riser shutoff fee, developer registration fee) not previously recorded in this store. See [[_Sources/YT_8zm_5NjbGNA_yana_vrublevskaya_minsk_mir_10k_studio|extraction note]] and [[11_Budget_and_Planning/case_studies/yana_vrublevskaya_minsk_mir_studio_2023_case|full case study]].

A concrete worked example shows AC/ventilation choice alone spanning ~5,000–50,000 RUB/m² for the same 100 m² apartment — folded into `12_Engineering_and_Systems/HVAC_and_Ventilation.md`. Full brand-by-category specification list (self-promotional, this studio's own choices) recorded in a companion case study, not imported into the guide. See [[_Sources/YT_6Z7uH2_rXsw_buro_segment_pricing_2026|extraction note]] and [[11_Budget_and_Planning/case_studies/buro_segment_pricing_2026_case|full case study]].

### Colour batch — 2026-08-30 (16 videos dispatched, 14 processed, 1 skipped, 1 already archived)

A batch dispatched specifically on **combining colours in interior design**. Preflight found 15 of the 16 fresh; `LRtedmEn8mQ` (Anuta Vlady) was already archived 2026-08-25 and was not re-fetched. All fetches were serialised at 75 s spacing with no rate-limit. This is `Design Concept`-taxonomy content, routed to `17_Design_and_Ergonomics/` rather than into this store's own sections, per that folder's routing rule. **No pricing content in any source** except one excluded promotional segment, so step 5b was not applicable across the batch. The colour topic outgrew its single page and was split into four — see that folder's Change Log for the structural decision, and its Source Notes for the per-source routing table.

- `yt_jOXPHR9Mpek` — "СОЧЕТАНИЕ ЦВЕТОВ В ИНТЕРЬЕРЕ - 2 ГЛАВНЫХ ПРАВИЛА ЗА 7 МИНУТ" (SHELNAT DESIGN / Наталья Шелковская, 2017-04-30, **channel confirmed via yt-dlp**) — **FULL extraction (fact_yield 4).** Match colours on equal **lightness and/or saturation**, not hue — with paint-fan-deck rows as the physical proxy, and a graded risk statement (the further across rows, the more you risk). Explicitly rejects forbidden-hue-pair thinking. Low promotional ratio. Note: this video's only *manual* caption track is English; it was deleted and re-fetched forcing `--languages ru` per the Russian-language-integrity rule. Routed to `Color_Harmony_and_Combination_Rules.md`. [[_Sources/YT_jOXPHR9Mpek_shelnat_two_color_combination_rules|note]].

- `yt_ZY_Xp8b_G5M` — "Сочетание цветов. Цветовые схемы и их использование" (Уроки рисования Artcolor.Online / Julia Omelchenko, 2019-06-22, **channel confirmed via yt-dlp**) — **FULL extraction (fact_yield 7).** Seven named colour harmonies with a **per-scheme interior verdict** (complementary→living rooms, analogous→bedrooms, split-complementary the most universal, square tetrad "на любителя", monochrome not recommended), plus the pick-the-main-colour-first prerequisite. Drawing-school source; the interior verdicts are the presenter's asides, not defended with interior examples. Its monochrome verdict is contradicted by three other sources in the same batch and is recorded as a Perspectives block, not a rule. Low promotional ratio. Routed to `Color_Harmony_and_Combination_Rules.md`. [[_Sources/YT_ZY_Xp8b_G5M_artcolor_color_harmony_schemes|note]].

- `yt_wjNz2wCtG7o` — "КОЛОРИСТИКА часть #1 Основы колористики" (Андрей Самарин, 2020-08-19, **channel confirmed via yt-dlp**) — **FULL extraction (fact_yield 4).** **Большой контраст / малый контраст (подбор на нюансе)** vocabulary, with interior application stated by the author himself; the **"волчок"** spinning-top balance model, which reaches 60/30/10's conclusion — unequal amounts — from wheel geometry instead; a perception-based argument for restraining the gamut; and a concrete description of over-applied nuance-matching in Soviet-era interiors. Low promotional ratio. Routed to `Color_Harmony_and_Combination_Rules.md`. [[_Sources/YT_wjNz2wCtG7o_samarin_colouristics_basics|note]].

- `yt_gEhVxuxtOjc` — "Как выбрать цвет для интерьера: почему вы не можете определиться" (Дизайнер интерьера Надежда Кузина, 2026-07-27, **channel confirmed via yt-dlp**) — **FULL extraction (fact_yield 6).** Why a technically correct palette still gets rejected: three mechanisms (**образы среды / социальные коды / личная память**), sourced to Pastoureau, Bourdieu and Itten, with a checkable historical counterexample (medieval red+green read as near-monochrome, yellow+green as hellish kitsch). Her stated intake order is **atmosphere → style → colour**, never "what colour do you like." Directly contradicts the per-hue colour-psychology genre; recorded as a Perspectives block against the existing Kodolov table rather than merged with it. Low promotional ratio. Routed to `Color_Selection_Process_and_Testing.md` and `Color_Palette_and_Material_Direction.md`. [[_Sources/YT_gEhVxuxtOjc_kuzina_why_you_cant_choose_a_color|note]].

- `yt_n5ZBqdq0wH8` — "Почему спокойные цвета делают интерьер грязным — и как правильно собрать Earthy Palette" (Дизайнер интерьера Надежда Кузина, 2026-08-24, **channel confirmed via yt-dlp**) — **FULL extraction (fact_yield 8).** **Calm palettes are harder to combine, not easier** — every shade sits close to every other, each carries a подтон that shifts with light, and there is no brightness to hide an error behind. Five-family earthy-palette taxonomy with explicit exclusions (streambed stone, not marble or onyx), the **"случайный гость"** membership test, a two-sided spacing rule (too similar gives грязь, so beginners should use more contrast inside the palette), and her stated control variable: **watch the lightness spread rather than the specific hue, and cap the count at two or three**. Low promotional ratio. Routed to `Neutrals_and_Earth_Tone_Palettes.md` and `Color_Selection_Process_and_Testing.md`. [[_Sources/YT_n5ZBqdq0wH8_kuzina_earthy_palette_practice|note]].

- `yt__Vw-eaRekDk` — "Самый простой способ подобрать цвета для интерьера" (Бюро ARCHWOOD / Марина Измайлова, 2025-10-26, **channel confirmed via yt-dlp**; manually-authored ru subtitles, the highest-fidelity transcript in the batch) — **FULL extraction (fact_yield 9).** **Selecting the palette is the easy half; distributing it is the hard half.** Ready-made palettes restated three years after `AKxOtRhIcJY`; the volume decision is independent of design training (her own rejected burgundy ceiling as the worked example); **household compromise named as the main reason palettes end up neutral**, from observing dozens of the bureau's own projects; colour-has-weight and the strip-out-the-decor composition acceptance test; complicate the base with half-tones and textures rather than adding colours; one palette per apartment with a widen-don't-add rule; and the batch's most distinctive new technique — **bold colour in short-dwell transit rooms, neutral in long-dwell rooms**, because a large volume of colour seen briefly doesn't have time to pall. Medium promotional ratio. Routed to `Color_Selection_Process_and_Testing.md`, `Color_Harmony_and_Combination_Rules.md`, `Whole_Apartment_Coherence.md`. [[_Sources/YT__Vw-eaRekDk_archwood_three_approaches_main_color|note]].

- `yt_AKxOtRhIcJY` — "Как подобрать цвет для интерьера со вкусом? Отвечаю на вопросы подписчиков" (Бюро ARCHWOOD / Марина Измайлова, 2022-08-27, **channel confirmed via yt-dlp**) — **FULL extraction (fact_yield 11).** The densest single source in the batch. Ready-made palettes and their structure (almost all built on opposing wheel positions plus a neutral); one palette → two opposite interiors via distribution (60–70% neutral for calm; neutral minimised to the floor for bold); **выкрасы mandatory even with a known colour code**; wood as a palette member by mentally stripping its texture; the vault's only full **window-orientation table** (east the largest distortion, complex grey-beiges the riskiest there, north the least distorting overall); a complete **ceiling** rule set including the ~3.2–3.5 m too-tall threshold and the paint-the-upper-wall-band execution rule; warm-advances/cool-recedes with an honest note on how small the effect is; the **"сложные цвета"** definition via a food analogy, and why you cannot mix one yourself; and two composition self-checks. Medium promotional ratio (an unrelated hotel-certificate sponsor read, disregarded). Routed to four pages. [[_Sources/YT_AKxOtRhIcJY_archwood_color_qa_orientation_ceilings|note]].

- `yt_jJ05zqugmSc` — "БЕЖЕВЫЙ в интерьере | Цветовые сочетания, серия 9" (Vittoria Selina / Вика, 2023-07-25, **channel confirmed via yt-dlp**) — **FULL extraction (fact_yield 12).** Definition of a **base/neutral beige** — mid-range and temperature-neutral, since a butter-leaning beige will not take a cool blue — with named Little Greene products (numbers flagged for re-verification, ASR). Two paths for a beige interior, the all-beige one carrying three rules (stretch across several lightnesses and explicitly don't colour-match swatches; texture difference mandatory; no coloured decor). Four named pairings including beige+blue with **black in details only**, quantity demonstrated by holding up a pencil. Beige/grey walls flatter objects better than white because they cast shade behind them. A **dissenting position that dark rooms should be painted dark**, recorded as a Perspectives block. Don't default a coloured room to a pure white ceiling — use a warm white/экрю. And a real client case behind **never judge wall paint before the interior is assembled**. Medium promotional ratio (a self-flagged webinar ad). Routed to four pages. [[_Sources/YT_jJ05zqugmSc_vittoria_selina_beige_combinations|note]].

- `yt_PGrxKyLhxp8` — "Делюсь секретами дизайнеров. Цвет в интерьере" (Anuta Vlady, 2024-03-10, **channel confirmed via yt-dlp**) — **FULL extraction (fact_yield 9).** Six named colour failure modes, each with a corrected version demonstrated on real photos: an accent repeated across **large** blocks stops being an accent (a distinct claim from Kruglov's repeat-at-least-three-times rule, which is about small objects — the two are explicitly kept apart on the destination page); adjacent large blocks merge; **warm/cool subtone mixing inside one apparent colour** (turquoise is a warm blue); **чистый vs. припылённый** matching; attention direction as a deliberate lever; and three accent-wall rules (darker than the others, one per room, placement justified by an object). Note the one-accent-wall rule already in the vault is from this same channel — a restatement, **not** independent corroboration. Low promotional ratio. Routed to `Color_Harmony_and_Combination_Rules.md` and `Decor_and_Finish_Selection_Technique.md`. [[_Sources/YT_PGrxKyLhxp8_anutavlady_typical_color_mistakes|note]].

- `yt_zF1YLMqIvNw` — "Универсальный цвет в интерьере на века! Правильно сочетаем бежевый и серый цвет в интерьере" (Школа дизайна Маши Черной, 2025-09-03, **channel confirmed via yt-dlp**) — **FULL extraction (fact_yield 7).** **Beige vs. grey decided by balance against the dominant material** — beige against a metal-and-glass-heavy interior, grey against a wood-heavy one; explicitly *not* decided by style or by material availability, though surface texture narrows it. Plus the batch's most actionable single rule, the **fan-deck lightness correction**: choose one to two tones lighter than the chip that appealed, because the deck is held perpendicular to the light and the same shade on a whole wall reads much darker and more saturated. And the **sample-juxtaposition** procedure that actually catches undertone clashes (doors, kitchen, выкрасы, flooring, skirting compared side by side). St Petersburg and Sochi named as daylight-availability contrasts — level 1, but climatic rather than jurisdictional, so the reasoning transfers to Belarus. Medium promotional ratio. Routed to `Neutrals_and_Earth_Tone_Palettes.md` and `Color_Selection_Process_and_Testing.md`. [[_Sources/YT_zF1YLMqIvNw_masha_chernaya_beige_vs_gray|note]].

- `yt_S9F9TGVG4x8` — "Всего ОДНО правило цвета в интерьере, которое меняет ВСЁ" (МЕБЕЛЬНЫЙ ТУР В КИТАЙ ОТ КОМПАНИИ PRIDE / Алёна, 2026-05-24, **channel confirmed via yt-dlp**) — **PARTIAL extraction (fact_yield 6).** The sixth independent statement of 60/30/10 in this vault, but with three things the existing entries lacked: it is a **hierarchy** claim rather than an arithmetic one, **neutrals don't count against the three-role budget** (the answer to "three colours isn't enough"), and an accent stops working at roughly **20–30%**. Plus the **"якорный предмет"** starting method — the third unrelated channel to arrive at deriving the palette from a multi-colour object rather than from wall paint — a retrofit path needing no repainting, and a diagnostic caution to check whether the problem is actually materials, proportion, light or form before reaching for colour. **High promotional ratio**: the embedded China-furniture-tour price comparisons are excluded as tier-steering, see `Advertising_Promotional_Notes.md`. Routed to `Color_Harmony_and_Combination_Rules.md` and `Color_Selection_Process_and_Testing.md`. [[_Sources/YT_S9F9TGVG4x8_pride_60_30_10_anchor_object|note]].

Within a pair, one colour deep and one warm/textured, only one of them the accent. And **a grey base takes exactly one added colour, not two or three**. The five named 2026 pairings are recorded as dated trend reference only, not adopted. High promotional ratio (scarcity-framed consultation offer). Routed to `Color_Harmony_and_Combination_Rules.md`. No rate-limit. **8 of 8 cleared the value bar** — the Group B verdict for this channel is a clear pass, and it is the first design channel in this vault to route substantially into `11_Budget_and_Planning`, `12_Engineering_and_Systems` and `13_Surfaces_and_Finishes` rather than only into `17_Design_and_Ergonomics`.

- `yt_avRNMkNdOBs` — "Ошибки в ремонте и дизайне интерьера мои топ 7" (Мария Шеврина / SMBUREAU, 2026-08-23, **channel confirmed via yt-dlp**) — **FULL extraction (fact_yield 7).** Seven mistakes, each with a mechanism. The headline item is a **controlled on-site experiment**: walls painted to a specific NCS article and a radiator panel made by their joiner to *exactly the same article* came out visibly different, the panel pinker — because the paint formulation, the substrate and the application method all differ. **The counter-intuitive fix: specify deliberately *different* shades to achieve a tone-on-tone match.** Also: never bridge laminate to walls with cork or sealant (in the manufacturers' own technical sheets; failure may not appear for years); push-to-open and cut-in pulls only on rarely-used fronts; **never hide an AC behind a perforated panel** (the unit's sensor sits inside the enclosure, so it satisfies itself at 18 °C while the room stays at 25 °C, plus condensation and mould within ~1–1.5 years); door swings should follow the actual approach direction; grey mottled tile reads as a dirty floor; and leave a plaster recess in advance so tile sits flush rather than gluing it over a finish. Low promotional ratio — unusually, no course pitch. [[_Sources/YT_avRNMkNdOBs_shevrina_top7_renovation_mistakes|note]].

- `yt_z4G-ocStu9o` — "Лучшие решения для интерьеров на примере реализованных ремонтов нашей студии" (Мария Шеврина / SMBUREAU, 2026-07-07, **channel confirmed via yt-dlp**) — **FULL extraction (fact_yield 10).** **A value-filter lesson worth recording: this title is exactly the shape the Group B criteria flag as a probable disguised showcase, and it is not one.** Ten named, generalised, reusable construction details: concealed curtain track for a bath curtain (with the how-to-remove-it answer); **recessed door frame, hinge side only or the handle hits the wall**; a second concealed interior door inboard of the entrance door (acoustic plus a psychological rationale); cabinet fronts in thin ≤6 mm large-format ceramic so wall and cabinet share one stone pattern; microcement over a heated wall as a drying wall in a utility block; **concealed shelf fixings — an acrylic or metal plate clamped between tile courses, no separate fixings at all**; shower glass in a concealed profile; porcelain in engineered-board plank formats to avoid a visible hall/living floor transition; filling a bath through the slot overflow instead of a protruding spout; and two lighting scenarios on one track. Low promotional ratio. [[_Sources/YT_z4G-ocStu9o_shevrina_ten_studio_detail_solutions|note]].

- `yt_QES02ExtmAg` — "Ложные антитренды в дизайне интерьера | Рейки скрытые двери и теневой плинтус" (Мария Шеврина / SMBUREAU, 2025-08-08, **channel confirmed via yt-dlp**) — **PARTIAL extraction (fact_yield 5).** The announced companion to `XGI6FS2ZdCc`; much of the runtime is reassurance rather than technique, so extraction was restricted. Real content: the marble format rule (avoid 20×40 and 30×60; go small or >120×60); microcement selection by **texture** rather than by material (smooth and calm lasts, mottled irritates); **co-planar sliding systems** described mechanically, with the caveat that they don't suit full-height wardrobes; and the actionable procurement instruction that **full-height sliding storage frontage should be ordered from a partition contractor, not a joinery shop**, because partition systems conceal the floor and ceiling rails. Two framing rules worth keeping: **\"людей тошнит не от мрамора, а от плохого дизайна вокруг этого мрамора\"** (a material blamed for a bad result is usually a scapegoat for the composition), and **any simplification or concealment of technical elements produces a durable interior**. Also: **MDF slat panels superseded by shadow profiles** on cost and technology grounds (the rhythm effect is fine, the product is the objection); the **yellow-walls failure mode of the north-room warming rule**; **independent corroboration that white/beige/greige are the *hardest* colours**, confirming Кузина's premise from an unrelated channel; plastic and metal trim replacements; multi-level ceilings; RGB strips; sofa beds versus a rolled 10 cm mattress; splayed legs; and the plant-as-universal-filler trick. Also: **shadow-gap profile at the floor/wall junction with real numbers** — a mop plus cloth is 10–15 mm high, so a 20 mm profile clears it, and a robot vacuum handles the dust objection — while a surface-mounted skirting is rated the *least* practical and **a flush MDF plinth is rated impractical too**, a finer three-way split than this vault held. Plus: paint window and entrance-door reveals rather than cladding them (damaged MDF is replaced, damaged paint is touched up), coarse-fraction microcement and lime-with-marble-chip as tougher alternatives, tile corner trims never justified, and **a blanket rejection of stretch ceilings** — recorded as a Perspectives block, since one half of that argument is contradicted by a documented flood-containment incident already on `Ceilings_Guide.md`. Low promotional ratio. [[_Sources/YT_0WXiKNXPD_0_shevrina_aesthetic_vs_practical|note]].

- `yt_b5oeFxmaubI` — "Белый цвет в интерьере | ДАЮ ИДЕАЛЬНЫЙ БЕЛЫЙ" (Мария Шеврина / SMBUREAU, 2023-04-23, **channel confirmed via yt-dlp**) — **FULL extraction (fact_yield 7).** Built around a satirical short she posted handing out invented \"best white\" article numbers, which viewers took seriously. **Their studio has never reused the same paint article on two objects** — the finished results read alike, but the articles differ, and two выкрасы side by side differ visibly in lightness, saturation and pigment. **Named failure case: Little Greene \"Flint\"**, a warm white she loved on one project which came out dirty grey-green on the next. Method: выкрасы with **two iterations, sometimes three**. **RAL is the wrong deck for wall colour** — it is for plastic and metal constructions; designers work from manufacturers' own decks (~99%) or NCS, which independently corroborates ARCHWOOD's \"сложные цвета\" argument. Routed partly as a **Perspectives block against Vittoria Selina** (`jJ05zqugmSc`), who names three Little Greene neutrals she reuses — same manufacturer, opposite methodological position. - `yt_UDrpyZE_V38` — "Как РЕАЛЬНО сэкономить на ремонте квартиры | Считаем деньги смотрим ведомость" (Мария Шеврина / SMBUREAU, 2023-02-20, **channel confirmed via yt-dlp**) — **FULL extraction (fact_yield 14).** **The strongest `11_Budget_and_Planning` source in the round** — a cost-structure analysis from the studio's own bills of quantities on a 90 m² minimalist turnkey project, not generic saving tips. **Contractor labour + rough materials = 24.5% of total, explicitly stated to repeat across projects**, and **the lever on it is the scope of work, not the choice of crew** (crew rates barely differ). Plasterboard partitions ~2.5× foam-block; **wall prep for painting is large enough that cladding a wall in 120×60 porcelain came out cheaper in labour than preparing it for paint** on one real project; joinery is the second-largest category and essentially irreducible; paint brand barely matters because labour dominates. Door cost levers with percentages: **reverse-swing +20%, floor-to-ceiling +15%, concealed doors no longer a premium**. Two figures: shadow-gap plinth profiles 44,000 RUB (≈$690) + installation 72,000 RUB (≈$1,100). Her \"useless economy\" list (plastic reveals, giving up concealed doors, cheap socket finishes, avoiding concealed mixers, avoiding big mirrors) and \"real economy\" list are both recorded. **Region unresolved at level 1** — RUB implies the Russian market but no city is named, so the absolute figures carry the standard comparability caveat while the proportions travel. Low promotional ratio. [[_Sources/YT_UDrpyZE_V38_shevrina_renovation_cost_structure|note]].

- `yt_NfHyCfo1J4w` — "Освещение в квартире | ОШИБКИ" (Мария Шеврина / SMBUREAU, 2022-07-24, **channel confirmed via yt-dlp**) — **FULL extraction (fact_yield 8).** The densest single-source lighting addition to `Lighting_Design.md` so far, with three numeric items. **Voltage drop: a reel is 5 m, and a run exceeding one reel fed from one end is visibly dimmer at the far end** — feed from two opposite corners, and give each of several strips its own cable rather than chaining them. **Luminous flux is independent of colour temperature** — two fixtures of equal lumens at 3000 K and 4000 K light a tabletop identically, and people downgrade to warm because they confuse *warm* with *soft*; if 4000 K is too bright, take 4000 K with fewer lumens. **Add 1 cm to a recessed fixture's stated depth** when calculating the ceiling drop. Also: don't trace a broken perimeter with LED strip; mount a visible strip to throw upward on a corner profile; switches with indicator lamps make spots glow or self-trigger (fix: shunt resistor); and size a magnetic track's power supply with headroom. Two points also routed to the colour pages: **neutral light doesn't distort the flat's colours**, and **the influence of a cornice strip on wall-colour choice is overstated**. Medium promotional ratio. [[_Sources/YT_NfHyCfo1J4w_shevrina_lighting_mistakes|note]].


### Мария Шеврина / SMBUREAU — Round 2 (2026-08-30, 8 videos)

Second round on this channel, scoped in `_Inbox/planning/shevrina_smbureau_channel_plan_20260830.md` after Round 1 returned 9.0 facts/video. All 8 fetched serialised at 75 s spacing with `--languages ru`, no rate-limit, none skipped. **Round 2 yield: 8 videos, 78 new facts, 9.75 per video** — slightly above Round 1, and confirming the channel is mis-classified as Group B.

**Structural outcome**: three of the eight are a loose-furniture cluster (sofa, tables, chairs) that crossed the 3-source page-creation threshold and produced this vault's **first loose-furniture pages** — `14_Furniture/Seating_and_Tables.md` plus three `analysis/` pages. `Furniture_Index.md` had explicitly anticipated exactly this gap. All three are the same channel, so the pages carry a prominent `single-account` caveat.

**A deliberate extraction decision worth recording**: the three furniture videos are largely brand-by-brand surveys of Russian furniture SKUs with per-model aesthetic verdicts. **None of those verdicts were recorded** — taste, market-specific, time-decaying, and failing this project's value criteria. Only the reasoning underneath was extracted, which is why ~55 minutes of video produced comparatively compact pages.

- `yt_GcTaKRQ3QC0` — "Как выбрать диван | Дизайн интерьера гостиной" (Мария Шеврина / SMBUREAU, 2024-09-24) — **PARTIAL extraction (fact_yield 8).** A brand-by-brand survey of Russian upholstered furniture — **no per-SKU verdict extracted**, only the reasoning underneath. Kept: **a bed, and any rest furniture, should not exceed 40 cm from the floor** (the only hard number in the seating sources); **a short bulky sofa makes a small room read smaller than a longer slim one**, caused by high seat height plus poor side-proportions and fixed by adding legs at the same sitting height; headboard height must be earned by length; **off-vertical elements clash with the room's real verticals** (door openings, panels, reveals, track lighting) — the best-argued rejection in the set; don't mix rectangular and round geometry in one piece; repetition of one form at several scales with identical radii; horizontals read as calm; fabric can cheapen or lift the same frame. Low promotional ratio but **the most taste-driven source from this channel so far**. [[_Sources/YT_GcTaKRQ3QC0_shevrina_choosing_a_sofa|note]].

- `yt_rRQ05JAoLtk` — "Как выбрать обеденные и кофейные столы в интерьер" (Мария Шеврина / SMBUREAU, 2024-06-02) — **FULL extraction (fact_yield 9).** **The dimensional core of the furniture cluster.** Журнальный (low table) **≤30–35 cm, large in plan**; кофейный/приставной **~50 cm+, small top, mobile** — two functionally different objects people conflate, and the named failure is one table that is both big and tall. Pairing convention: **one bigger-and-lower plus one smaller-and-higher**, not a matched set; worked sizing 32 cm × 90 cm dia plus a 45 cm. **Co-scale rule: a small side table suits a ~180–190 cm sofa and reads wrong against a 2,400–2,500 mm one.** Massive-base tables held to 30–35 cm max. Plus the **\"why a piece reads designed\" causes list** — coaxiality, repetition at scale, deliberate scale contrast, **equal corner radii at every junction** — and **contrast of forms as the visual-weight lever** (thick base + thin top reads light). Low promotional ratio. [[_Sources/YT_rRQ05JAoLtk_shevrina_choosing_dining_and_coffee_tables|note]].

- `yt_ccZBjb5qIQA` — "Стулья для кухни | Как выбрать стул в столовую зону" (Мария Шеврина / SMBUREAU, 2023-06-25) — **PARTIAL extraction (fact_yield 6).** Third of the furniture cluster; brand survey again, verdicts not extracted. **Its value is sharpening the leg rule past the slogan: the objection is to legs raked in *two* directions at once — \"назад было бы достаточно\". A backward rake alone is fine; the simultaneous sideways splay is the failure.** Also: **legs must be visually anchored** — the rake should coincide with the back's angle, with coaxially aligned parts, or the chair reads as \"винегрет из деталей\"; junction quality as the tell; bulk vs room size; a cheap light-wood seat glued from offcuts betrays itself and a dark finish hides it; **saturated colours lower perceived status**; mid-century as the safe thing to mix into minimalism, but only with period support (lighting, low tables, glass and chromed metal). Her own top criterion recorded as-is: \"если вам стул нравится — значит это хороший стул.\" Low promotional ratio. [[_Sources/YT_ccZBjb5qIQA_shevrina_choosing_dining_chairs|note]].

- `yt_KI2GvB0jzHs` — "ПОДКАСТ 01 Ремонт | Дизайн кухни | Какую столешницу выбрать | Как подобрать цвет" (Мария Шеврина / SMBUREAU, 2023-03-18) — **FULL extraction (fact_yield 12).** **The richest source of Round 2, and a new format for this channel** — live running commentary on a real project rather than a polished explainer, which surfaces procurement constraints an explainer never mentions. **Completes the Round 1 разнотон finding**: facade samples must be real MDF-in-enamel fronts, not paint on paper, at **~3,000 RUB (≈$50) each** — cheap enough to order several; plus the **sequencing rule that the less flexible material constrains the more flexible one** (choose the stone slab, then eliminate the enamel shades that don't suit it). Also: **the fabricator's capabilities are a hard design constraint** — a client-chosen joinery shop that doesn't do ceramic killed ceramic on the island and fronts outright; **material-availability risk scales inversely with how common the decor is** (popular decors have substitutes, onyx does not — reserve and warehouse anything rare); **up to ~1.5 years between design and joinery realisation** is why renders and results diverge; two microcement-on-fronts execution routes, with the same-applicator route required when a wall in the room is also microcement; and ceramic vs acrylic worktop tradeoffs with a clean stated principle — **specify for the realistic owner, not the ideal one**. Low promotional ratio. [[_Sources/YT_KI2GvB0jzHs_shevrina_podcast01_kitchen_material_procurement|note]].

- `yt_6y3UiXx9NQI` — "Дизайн кухни в современных интерьерах: ошибки" (Мария Шеврина / SMBUREAU, 2024-04-17) — **FULL extraction (fact_yield 13).** **The most numerically dense kitchen source in this vault**, and the figures cross-check rather than being asserted separately. **Dining table to wall 75 cm = 45 (seated) + 30 (passing sideways); to a kitchen run 110 cm = 50 (seated) + 60 (front opening); 145 cm with a dishwasher open plus a non-tucking chair.** A dishwasher door occupies a full **80 cm**. Measure to the chairs when they don't tuck. **Fridge needs a 7–8 cm filler strip** or the crisper drawers won't pull out — and the shift can strand an already-installed socket. **A radiator within 60 cm of the run blocks the fronts.** **Stone fabricators require a 10–15 cm hob setback from the edge or they won't warranty the worktop.** Plus a **third documented experiment** — an overhang *with a drip groove* still fails to keep water off the fronts, extending the Round 1 overhang test; the mechanism behind the push-to-open objection (two motions, not one); stainless over acrylic sinks; sockets inside cabinets; and worktop-shadow lighting with **60° spots vs ~90–110° diffuse**. **Contains a Russian-law claim** (shaft-vented hoods now illegal) explicitly kept out of `16_Legal_and_Regulations/`. Low promotional ratio. [[_Sources/YT_6y3UiXx9NQI_shevrina_kitchen_design_mistakes|note]].

- `yt_haM4H-b-bZM` — "Какую плитку выбрать в санузел | Тренды и вечно актуальные виды плиток" (Мария Шеврина / SMBUREAU, 2024-05-09) — **FULL extraction (fact_yield 11).** Deepens the Round 1 tile-format rules with causes and a cost. **The \"dirty tile\" effect has three causes — too mottled, too dark, wrong (especially cold) undertone — intensified by pairing with saturated warm wood; plus a fourth physical cause, printed darkened edges making every unit's border visible.** **Showroom test: lay 2–3 tiles of the collection side by side** and check for edge darkening and whether a continuous texture is achievable. **Marble must be matte** — \"в природе не встречаются полированные материалы\" — with the small classic format the one place gloss works. Mid formats (20×40, 30×60) are the problem; marble from **120×45** upward. Relief taxonomy (slat/rib timeless; representational, complex-geometric and wave reliefs rejected). Horizontal two-tile wall splits defended as **not** an anti-trend. **And the standout: a 3 m slab doesn't fit in a lift — hand-carry runs 30,000–50,000 RUB (≈$330–$550), avoidable by having the slab cut at the factory to ~1 m pieces with the cuts aligned to real design lines.** Low promotional ratio. [[_Sources/YT_haM4H-b-bZM_shevrina_bathroom_tile_selection|note]].

- `yt_MOXm91CE-QQ` — "Как продумать дизайн ПРИХОЖЕЙ в стиле минимализм" (Мария Шеврина / SMBUREAU, 2023-05-07) — **FULL extraction (fact_yield 10).** **Raise the entrance wardrobe 30–40 cm off the floor** (15 cm minimum) — their near-universal technique, with five cumulative reasons: what's underneath is invisible from the flat, low shoes live there, **a robot-vacuum dock fits**, underfloor heating can dry wet shoes there, and a pouffe can be pushed under. Two caveats: the back panel may not carry the load (ask the joinery shop), and choose the pouffe first. Plus a **three-level ladder for lightening the entrance door** — flat panel in the wall colour; a contrasting finish **only if carried to the ceiling** or it reads as a patch; or a second concealed interior door, since \"у них скрыты лишь петли\" on a concealed entrance door. **Heating manifolds are the hard engineering case** — the raised wardrobe does not hide their feed pipes; recess them, or pass through to the adjacent room and reinforce the opening where you can't lose ~12 cm. Contains a **Russian БТИ-designation claim** about entrance-zone water supply, kept out of `16_Legal_and_Regulations/`. **Also a methodological aside worth keeping**: asked how to protect door reveals, she questions whether the problem is real and says \"не берите на веру любые советы из интернета — даже мои.\" Medium promotional ratio. [[_Sources/YT_MOXm91CE-QQ_shevrina_minimalist_hallway_design|note]].

- `yt_dSm7M6oGFis` — "Ошибки в дизайне интерьеров | Разбираю интерьеры подписчиков" (Мария Шеврина / SMBUREAU, 2024-02-14) — **FULL extraction (fact_yield 9).** **Included as a format probe — the subscriber-critique format was the most substantive one on Anuta Vlady's channel — and it passes.** Every item is a named problem on a real submitted interior with a specific corrective and a stated reason. **Staggered-height pendants named as the most frequent mistake she sees**, same for staggered shelves. **The over-wide bath alcove: building out only ONE side is the mistake** — either build both sides shallower with a full-length horizontal shelf and a dropped ceiling painted the same colour, or build a **60 cm tiled podium larger than the bath with margins at both ends**. Paint all three alcove walls one colour so it \"окутывает\" the bath. Niches should be thin, not massive. **Shelf corrective with a dimension: a ~2 cm central vertical divider, fewer and thinner horizontals.** **Square floor drains rejected on a fabrication ground invisible in a render — the floor must fall to the drain, so the tile gets cut heavily including diagonals.** Knotty high-contrast wood-look works only on floors, not walls. Medium promotional ratio. **Two more videos of this format exist (`A5G04uPvzL0`, `p5lXLETWI5s`) and are now worth processing.** [[_Sources/YT_dSm7M6oGFis_shevrina_subscriber_interior_critiques|note]].


### Мария Шеврина / SMBUREAU — Round 3 (2026-08-31, 8 videos)

Third round, selected **entirely by format rather than topic** on the evidence of two prior rounds: the podcast, mistakes and subscriber-critique formats carry mechanisms and numbers, while brand surveys are half aesthetic verdict and yield least. Preflight confirmed 16 duplicates correctly deduped and **no PODCAST 02 exists** (the listing is still 37 videos), so that planned item was dropped and the slot given to the bedroom video. All 8 fetched serialised at 75 s spacing with `--languages ru`, no rate-limit, none skipped.

**Round 3 yield: 8 videos, 88 new facts, 11.0 per video** — up again from Round 2's 9.75 and Round 1's 9.0. **Three rounds, 24 videos, no decay.**

**Three corrections to earlier rounds came out of this one**, which is the routing pass working as intended rather than merely placing content:

1. **The stretch-ceiling Perspectives block on `Ceilings_Guide.md` was substantially revised.** It recorded her flood argument as weak (correctly) and her sequencing point as her only strong one. `TUVsZ1Xx1aQ` supplies a third argument that is stronger than either — **manufacturers publish 2.5% sag, giving ~7 cm at room centre on a room over 8 m²** — and the arithmetic cross-checks. The original assessment understated her case and now says so explicitly.

2. **A numeric conflict was found and flagged, not silently resolved**: reverse-swing doors are **+20%** in Round 2's bill-of-quantities video and **~+30%** here. Both recorded; the cost-structure video noted as better-evidenced; and the point that survives either way is the **ordering** — a floor-to-ceiling door is a smaller premium than a reverse-swing one.

3. **A thickness inconsistency flagged in Round 2 is resolved**: thin large-format ceramic was given as "up to 6 mm" in one video and "3–5 mm" in another; `nYvqqkNeS4k` gives "~3–6 mm, slabs ~3 m × 1 m", which reconciles them.

**A new Perspectives block** on `Neutrals_and_Earth_Tone_Palettes.md` records a genuine four-way disagreement about **how many tones of one colour** an interior should carry — Shevrina states the low-count position three separate times in this round, against Vittoria Selina and Студия Wall Street from the colour batch, with Надежда Кузина between the poles. Scope (architectural minimalism; small flats) may explain much of it, and that is said rather than assumed.

- `yt_AFBu0Uv-47U` — "Плохой дизайнер | Как дизайнеры обманывают заказчиков" (2023-08-28) — **FULL (fact_yield 9).** **The first source in this vault aimed at helping a client evaluate a designer.** Two kickback schemes: **invoice padding at 15-20%** (she calls it fraud, says it is actionable in court) and a **concealed discount split** (client sees 5-10% while the designer keeps the rest of a 25% trade discount). **The client-side defence: never let a designer make purchases through themselves - payment goes client-to-shop directly.** Three questions to ask at the first meeting (how are discounts split, what is the payment scheme for services and procurement, is it in the contract). Two competence markers: a designer who publicly recommends paint article numbers is incompetent (**she implicates her own past self**), and a designer should decline a budget they can't work with rather than render unobtainable furniture. Careful to separate malpractice from legitimate divergence (client-chosen fabricator, supply disruption, currency moves) - all three corroborating the Round 2 podcast. [[_Sources/YT_AFBu0Uv-47U_shevrina_bad_designer_kickbacks|note]].

- `yt_TUVsZ1Xx1aQ` — "Плохие решения в ремонте | Ошибки в ремонте" (2023-07-09) — **FULL (fact_yield 12).** **Supplies the argument that forced a revision of the stretch-ceiling Perspectives block on `Ceilings_Guide.md`.** Round 1 recorded her flood argument as the weak half and her sequencing point as her only strong one; **she now gives a third, stronger, manufacturer-sourced argument: sag is published at 2.5%, and on a room exceeding 8 m2 centre sag reaches 7 cm** - independent of installer skill or membrane price, worst at spot and track penetrations. **The arithmetic cross-checks** (2.5% of ~2.8 m = 70 mm). Also: **finishes before furniture as an irreversibility-ranked budget rule**; a real economy marker (porcelain at ~1,000 RUB/m2 on a rental project, but still no stretch ceiling and no wallpaper); wallpaper cannot be restored and **accentuates cracks rather than hiding them**; **cement grout cracks over a heated floor or wall**; and a full four-part account of why reverse-swing doors are avoided - **including a 30% figure that conflicts with Round 2's 20%, flagged rather than reconciled.** [[_Sources/YT_TUVsZ1Xx1aQ_shevrina_bad_renovation_decisions|note]].

- `yt_Bnuim5NjgCU` — "УЖАСНЫЕ РЕШЕНИЯ в минимализме ТОП 10" (2023-03-30) — **FULL (fact_yield 11).** Three separable objections to **L- and U-shaped kitchens** (perceptual; material joins fail at the internal corner; the blind corner is never fully usable) - and **the correction worth having: whether an island works depends on the flat's configuration, not its area** (a 37 m2 flat took one; a 70 m2 flat may not). **'Fifty shades of beige'** - varying tones within one hue to avoid boredom produces bad taste, not minimalism; **first of three statements of a tone-count position that conflicts with two sources already in this vault.** **One wood tone only**, explicitly rejecting the common 'up to three is fine' advice. **Black accessories: the quantity is the mistake, not the colour** - manufacturers now issue black as a second base colour, so people over-buy and a 4 m2 bathroom fills with small visual noise. **The shadow-gap dust objection inverted** (dust settles on a surface-mounted skirting too, and there you wipe it by hand). **Tile trims rejected with new evidence** - flush details are standard abroad and in developers' own common areas, and she reports hers are durable. **And storage planned for the future, not the present** - the vacuum, the child, the pet, the hobby returned to. [[_Sources/YT_Bnuim5NjgCU_shevrina_worst_minimalism_decisions|note]].

- `yt_nYvqqkNeS4k` — "ДИЗАЙН СПАЛЬНИ в минимализме | Освещение | Акцентная стена" (2023-06-11) — **FULL (fact_yield 13).** **The densest single-source lighting addition this vault has had.** Three obligatory bedroom scenarios, with the chandelier-alone argument rejected (misses the corners) but no-ceiling-light legitimised where the room is only slept in. **The headboard LED strip must throw light into the ceiling from a corner profile on a plasterboard backing** - the usual cut-a-niche-and-glue approach glares once you lean back. **Evening light not above 3000 K.** **And the distinction that makes the source valuable: diffuse light cannot be a night light** - it wakes the person beside you; for night use and for reading in bed you need a **directional beam up to ~60 deg** lighting only a small patch. **Floor-lamp switching must be planned** (socket with integral switch, or wire the low socket to a bedside wall switch). Plus a **headboard-wall material taxonomy** that **resolves the Round 2 ceramic-thickness inconsistency at ~3-6 mm, slabs ~3 m x 1 m**; don't accent both headboard and opposite wall; **a soft headboard plus cushions eats 25-30 cm**, so consider a headboard-less bed with wall panels instead. [[_Sources/YT_nYvqqkNeS4k_shevrina_bedroom_lighting_and_headboard|note]].

- `yt_9h2tAnm6rqA` — "Как сделать дизайн маленькой квартиры | Приемы и лайфхаки" (2023-09-12) — **FULL (fact_yield 12).** **Substantially extends the mirror rules already on `Color_Palette_and_Material_Direction.md`**, which had two conditions and now has four: a small mirror doesn't enlarge, several small mirrors actively clutter, **oval and framed mirrors read as decor rather than as an optical device**, only floor-to-ceiling works - **and don't centre it on a wall: set it into an internal corner so one edge butts the perpendicular wall, or place it at a window.** **The counter-intuitive centrepiece: furniture toward the centre of the flat gives more sense of space than furniture against the walls**, because clear walls let you read their full height and verticals are what produce the sense of volume - which is also why an island lightens a small kitchen. Plus: avoid active textures (white marble with contrasting veining, mottled concrete); **the smaller the flat the fewer tones, and for a very small flat one colour in one tone**; two or three materials maximum with рейки as the single decorative device; less decor, and stop filling every horizontal surface; **black ceiling fixtures and tracks are wrong in a small flat** - use white, recessed, ideally trimless. **Three myths rejected**: niches don't shrink a flat, furniture on legs is not required to lighten it, and mirrors/white walls are real but banal. [[_Sources/YT_9h2tAnm6rqA_shevrina_small_apartment_techniques|note]].

- `yt_HImsHPnSh-c` — "Дизайн ванной в минимализме: нетипичные решения" (2023-10-30) — **FULL (fact_yield 10).** **A composition-based defence of microcement in wet areas** - it is cement + polymers + quartz sand, quartz being what makes a quartz worktop durable, and it is used on walls and floors in public high-traffic spaces; **it can also go on the ceiling**, which is how you get a single-finish box effect where porcelain is too heavy. **Mixer and shower placement by scenario rather than by reference photo** - hand shower and its mixer on the long wall centred on the bath so you don't reach across the screen; overhead on the short wall but **its mixer on the long wall** so you can warm the water before stepping in - and she raises the counter-argument against her own suggestion. **Concealed-profile glass screens: procurement and timing** - if a showroom can't do it, leave; it is not the builders' job; **raise it before tiling, because installation may be two-stage**; and on a bath the glass sits on the rim through sealing tape, never bare. **Rectangular over square tile on walls**, with a perceptual argument she also applies to pillows and screens. Plus a heated wall behind the mirror against condensation, and shelf placement by sightline. [[_Sources/YT_HImsHPnSh-c_shevrina_minimalist_bathroom_solutions|note]].

- `yt_A5G04uPvzL0` — "Разбор интерьера подписчицы" (2024-01-12) — **FULL (fact_yield 9).** Second of the two remaining critique-format videos, and **it turns into the full mid-century component checklist** that Round 2's chairs video only gestured at: chromed metal, glass, **clean saturated colours in small doses - an explicit scoped exception to her own rule that saturated colours cheapen**, wood in saturated greenish tones, named furniture archetypes, **specific plant species (olive for strict minimalism; ficus lyrata or monstera for mid-century)**, metal shelves and heavy commodes. **A hard number: a dining pendant should end 75-80 cm above the tabletop, ~1.5 m from the floor** - which cross-checks against the 150-170 cm island figure in the companion critique. Also: unify wood tones and metal finishes; **textile softens corners and adds warmth even where light control isn't needed**, and matters most over a hard floor like microcement; **curtains and panels are 'clean-stage' fixes** still available after the renovation is finished; and **a dark wall recedes and becomes background** - a mechanism for using dark colour to reduce prominence rather than create an accent. **Format constraint worth noting: she limits herself to changes that don't require demolishing finishes**, which makes the advice unusually practical. [[_Sources/YT_A5G04uPvzL0_shevrina_subscriber_critique_midcentury|note]].

- `yt_p5lXLETWI5s` — "Разбор интерьера подписчицы | Дизайн интерьера в стиле минимализм" (2023-05-16) — **FULL (fact_yield 12).** **Finally supplies the perceptual mechanism behind the flush-junction rule this studio has asserted across five videos**: a thin surface-glued overlay reads as an *error*, because the brain registers the small offset as a construction defect - 'вроде попадаешь, но вроде не попадаешь'. **The fix is an either/or: land it perfectly flush, or deliberately separate it 10-15 cm so alignment clearly wasn't attempted.** The near-miss is what fails. **Scoped exception: this does not apply to mirrors.** Also: **track quantity matters** (parallel lines, not a square of tracks); **spots at 40-45 cm from the wall where upper cabinets are shallow**, a far more specific fix for worktop shadow than Round 2 had; **three valid resolutions for two pendant zones**; **island pendant down to 150-170 cm**; don't wrap a material around internal corners; don't put two materials in one plane; **wood grain should follow the object's proportions**; and **the undertone corrective** - an active concrete texture plus a neutral or cool undertone in a warm interior produces the dirt effect, fixed by warming both the concrete and the chair fabric. Plus the awkward-pier-between-two-windows treatments and the transom-panel workaround for doors that can't be full height. [[_Sources/YT_p5lXLETWI5s_shevrina_subscriber_critique_minimalism|note]].

- `yt_k1YBZzIMAgw` — "Стиль МУДЖИ" (2024-01-21) — **PARTIAL (fact_yield 9) — a taxonomy source, and the weakest of the 25 processed from this channel.** No prices, no dimensions, no procurement detail, no failure modes; seven of nine items are style definition or aesthetic doctrine. **Two items justify the note.** First, **a style-level cost floor**: minimalism can be executed cheaply with imitation materials and still read correctly, **muji cannot** — quality *is* the style there — and **scandi survives a developer's base finish**. That gives an asserted (entirely uncosted) floor ordering scandi → minimalism → japandi/wabi-sabi → muji, and it bounds the per-system tier framework from above, because a style choice can remove the cheap tier from several systems at once. Second, **a fourth statement of her one-wood-tone rule** (muji: wood in light oak only), which strengthens the evidence she holds it consistently but, being scoped to one named style, supports the *scope* explanation in the tone-count Perspectives block rather than her side of it. Also usable: **a compatibility claim with a mechanism** — muji combines with scandi, wabi-sabi and mid-century, not with classic or art deco, because the clash is at the level of philosophy rather than appearance, which supplies the missing "why" behind this store's existing ~3-direction eclectic mixing cap. **ASR mangles every style name and the brand names; one brand was omitted rather than guessed.** [[_Sources/YT_k1YBZzIMAgw_shevrina_muji_style_differentiation|note]].


- `yt_iHViNm3dESU` — "Как правильно сделать электрику и освещение на кухне: розетки, подсветка и электровыводы" (2021-09-26, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 27).** A ~47-minute recorded seminar and **the densest single kitchen-electrics source this vault has**, most of it spent disagreeing with the socket-layout diagrams that dominate search results. **The most useful finding is a negative one**: she searched ГОСТ/СНиП for rules on outlet *position* and found almost nothing — the norms mostly require earthing and three phases, so **most placement "rules" online are convention, not code.** What she could substantiate (RU jurisdiction, deliberately kept out of `16_Legal_and_Regulations/`): 50 cm from a gas pipe, one outlet per 3 m of perimeter, none under or over the sink, 60 cm from sink and hob. **She then resolves the under-sink prohibition against reality** — everyone fits a disposer and there is often no neighbouring base — choosing the sink zone over the plinth, splash-proof and mounted high. **Opens a genuine Perspectives disagreement on countertop outlet height: she gives 100 cm against this vault's existing 110 cm**, arguing compositionally (at 130 cm it lands mid-backsplash) and rejecting the «уголок»-clearance argument as irrational. **Two named transferable methods**: leave an electrical stub and fit a *surface-mounted* outlet inside the hood casing once the hood physically arrives, because casings differ in their fixings; and turn a cabinet back into a **hatch («лючок»)** with fixed shelves above and below so the plug is never seen. **Carcass-referenced heights** the floor-plane figures don't cover: **250 mm minimum inside a sink base** — a correction to her own 200 mm mistake that collided with the base's internal stiffener rail — 25 cm behind a freestanding freezer, and the arithmetic showing **a 10 cm plinth leaves ~1 cm of faceplate clearance** once floor tolerance is counted. **Independently corroborates 4000 K worktop task light** from a channel unrelated to Kruglov, and adds the constraint that matters more: white task light under warm ambient reads as a mistake, which is this vault's same-scene colour-temperature rule arriving from a second direction. Plus **two stubs at the outer extremes** where the run splits at the dish rack and hood; the stub goes high (into the wall-unit tops or behind the cornice) with ~1.5 m slack; **plinth lighting rejected outright on function** — it rakes the floor and shows every crumb; **never put the hood outlet on the ducting centreline**; **side-by-side rejected on a hinge, not a clearance** (68–70 cm deep, and the door is designed assuming it protrudes, so deepening the cabinets doesn't fix it); **upper cabinets are 32/35/37 cm and many microwaves simply won't fit**; and a wall switch **plus** proximity sensor as the working alternative to a bare touch sensor. Prices are 2021 RUB, converted. **ASR quality poor** — several numeric passages were deliberately not extracted rather than guessed. [[_Sources/YT_iHViNm3dESU_kuzina_kitchen_electrics_and_lighting|note]].

- `yt_g252JnMcc3Q` — "Четыре способа как стыковать напольные покрытия разных видов" (2020-02-05, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 9).** An 8.5-minute four-option comparison ending in a stated recommendation — the "N options for one problem, with reasoning" shape this project's value filter favours. **Makes this vault's cork-vs-threshold finding a three-channel convergence** (Kruglov/Ontario and Шеврина/SMBUREAU already agreed), but **the verdict is not the contribution — the positive condition is.** Cork, sealant and grout are not simply wrong; they are wrong **for a floating floor**, and she uses rigid-joint methods routinely on bonded floors. **The genuinely new item is the partial-bond trick**: where the floor must float, bond only the single row that meets the tile, so the rest moves as designed while the joint row stays stable. **Also the one geometry that rules thresholds out entirely** — a deliberately irregular joint (hexagonal tile interlocking into parquet), where a threshold cannot follow the line and grout is the answer. Plus: liquid cork is sealant with cork crumb, **and at the recommended ≤5 mm joint width the crumb sometimes will not fit through**; plain silicone is the most elastic and least visible but hardens over time and is easily picked out (a pet acquiring the habit); the metal threshold works with a floating floor if its cap exceeds ~5 mm, and **its real obstacle is procurement — it cannot be bought with the tile or with the parquet and is the item most likely to be missing on site.** **Separately supplies the build-up figures the screed decision turns on: tile + adhesive ≈15 mm against parquet + plywood + adhesive ≈30–32 mm**, which puts numbers on Петришин-Строй's qualitative thickness-direction asymmetry. And the joint must land under the door leaf, so swing direction is an input to the screed programme. No prices. [[_Sources/YT_g252JnMcc3Q_kuzina_flooring_transition_methods|note]].

- `yt_fSBAMSIgnBs` — "Что делать с выступающим углом. Как сделать износостойкий угол" (2020-04-15, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 11).** **Thirteen named solutions to one problem with a stated reason for or against each** — precisely the shape this project's Group B triage criteria single out as genuinely reusable, and the reason the count is high despite the source containing **no dimensions, no prices and no measured claims at all.** **Reframes the inward-pointing corner as a wear problem before an aesthetic one** — clothing and elbows abrade it — and classifies the geometry itself as a defect: "это ошибка архитектуры, с которой нужно бороться." **Seven cladding options ranked worst-first**: plastic/metal corner protectors are worst unconditionally, and **she names why they appear — builders use them to avoid having to get the corner straight**; ragged-edge decorative brick is second worst and **in a narrow corridor it inverts the problem, since you then abrade yourself on the slate**; a pilaster's *material* decides whether it solves anything (**joinery is wipe-clean like a cabinet corner, polyurethane wears at the bare corner's own rate**), and **its strongest argument is renewal — repaint the pilaster instead of re-hanging wallpaper on both walls** — with the easily-missed detail that it needs a plinth block («каблучок») for the baseboard to die into; tile-system metal trims are acceptable where a bare wall corner protector is not; liquid wallpaper is genuinely spot-restorable but she rates it office-grade; and **paintable wallpaper will show the touch-up even with the original paint.** **Six geometry-changing options**: chamfer; radius (**must be justified by other curves, and it changes the adjacent room too**); niches with shelves, whose mechanism is behavioural — you fear knocking things off; an obstacle in front, which **fails with children or dogs**; a column, with the old-Moscow carriage guard-stone as the analogy; and **her own default, splitting the room with a deliberately wide cased opening** — "не такой проём, который размером с дверь, а большой широкий" — which also permits a wall-colour change between the two parts while keeping them fully open to each other. **The architectural term for the corner was garbled by ASR and deliberately omitted rather than guessed.** [[_Sources/YT_fSBAMSIgnBs_kuzina_protruding_corner_protection|note]].

- `yt_Rcd9gkPC6CI` — "Эргономика квартиры: Как самостоятельно сделать планировку спальни, детской и прихожей" (2020-10-27, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 38). A 78-minute lecture with ~20 minutes of live Q&A, and the largest single planning-method source in this vault.** **The Q&A is the densest part**, because the questions force specific numbers out of her. **⚠️ The single most useful item is a correction to a source this vault has been implicitly relying on**: the ergonomic figures everyone googles come from **Нойферт (Neufert)**, still valid — **but they are the minimum tolerable, not the comfortable. Add 10%, better 15%, better 20%.** And where two sources disagree on a figure, take the larger. **One number covers most cases: 1–1.2 m.** **Her central technique is the verb list** — write what you actually *do* in the room, then filter it twice against what you want to stop doing (her example: coffee in bed is a symptom of a one-person kitchen table, a process to design *out*) and what you want to start, then decompose each verb into light, acoustic and sightline requirements. **Planning order is tasks → constraints → furniture**, and "we need a corner sofa, where does it go" is a good question asked at the wrong point. **She argues *for* walk-through rooms against universal client resistance** — a central corridor in a 100 m² flat can eat ~15 m², a whole room's worth — attributing the resistance to a residual fear of Soviet «уплотнение» and to the once-a-year visiting relative. **Room shape cannot be fixed with finishes or rearrangement**; axes are what tell you where the centre is, and **she advises against fully asymmetric layouts for a striking reason — more options is not freedom, it is more choices, and it ends in worse work.** **Diagnostic for an unfixable plan**: a Houzz layout where the circulation zone is 14 m² against children's rooms of 14 and 11 — circulation larger *and* better-proportioned than either habitable room, at which point "дизайнер бессилен." **Hard numbers throughout**: bed ≠ mattress (160×200 mattress → 180×210, better 180×220, because of the царга and an angled headboard); 5–10 cm between nightstand and bed; **chest depth + 60 cm** and **wardrobe leaf + 60 cm** as clearance formulas, each with the arithmetic shown; 70 cm around a bed is Neufert's minimum and "очень мало"; a built-in wardrobe's dropped ceiling must be set out to **70 cm, not 60**, because of the cornice overhang; **dressing room entered from the end needs 142 cm absolute minimum** (60 wardrobe + 72 opening + 10 architrave), or enter from the long side and treat it as a very deep wardrobe with two side wings for the vacuum and ironing board; cutting a dressing room off a room needs 1.7–1.8 m and should be done to an elongated room, never a good square one. **Kids' room**: a full safety checklist (tilt windows key-lockable, no corded blinds, shuttered sockets, furniture fixed to the wall), **the highest-leverage tip in the source — lay the electrics for the child's *second* layout now, so the age-7 change is cosmetic only**; the darkest corner goes to the bed and the lightest to study; **girls need larger beds than boys because they play on them**; themed beds constrain imagination and are tired of within days; **podium beds abandoned as a plan** (few joinery shops can do it, and it depends on windowsill height); and **bunk beds need a two-way switch at the door — she tested the failure and does not fit on the ladder.** **Hallway**: a buffer zone where social roles change, hence the mirror; bolder treatment is affordable because it is a pass-through; a well-planned one routes a guest to the living room unprompted; and **from habitable rooms you should not see the WC or hallway**, because coats end up on hooks and shoes drip snow. **⚠️ She pushes back on "light colours enlarge a room" as a cliché** — saturated colours do eat space, but dark rooms often read well, pass-through zones are *better* dark, and colour only oppresses when uniform. **RU-jurisdiction constraints kept out of `16_Legal_and_Regulations/`**: habitable rooms are limited by window count not area; wet zones and gas kitchens are effectively immovable; entrance-door swing must not block a neighbour's fire exit; the entrance door cannot be relocated at all. **Also records a practitioner declining to solve a problem** — a 2.70 × 4.70 m bedroom, where after three options she says "я просто не знаю, как сделать так, чтобы из 2,70 было бы 3,70." **Two of her own worktop figures differ between this source and `iHViNm3dESU`** (84–85 cm vs 815–820 mm), reconciled on `Kitchen_Furniture.md` rather than resolved. **She also takes a personal swipe at Земсков**, this vault's largest technical source — recorded as professional rivalry, not a technical disagreement. No prices anywhere. [[_Sources/YT_Rcd9gkPC6CI_kuzina_apartment_ergonomics_bedroom_kids_hallway|note]].

- `yt_B7G_oKHEjkc` — "Натуральное дерево в ванной комнате" (2023-01-31, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 19). This vault's first source on wood in a wet room, and the most technically substantive of Round 1 after the two long lectures** — unusually for this channel a *materials* source rather than a planning one, and framed sceptically: "давайте разбираться, что из этого просто красивая картинка, а что имеет связь с реальностью." **Two preconditions frame everything: good ventilation, and impregnated wood** — and **distance from wet zones is a planning-stage decision, with the explicit warning that dry zones still suffer condensate.** **Species ranked with reasons, not preferences: oak is best because it contains tannin, a natural antiseptic that inhibits fungal growth** (not merely because it is dense); **beech is popular for furniture and must not go in a bathroom** — it bows and opens along its glue lines, or cracks; ash is harder than some oaks but blotchy, so usually toned; larch is the cheap option that works given proper anti-rot impregnation; **birch is a scoped exception — it cracks fast, which is a defect everywhere except a wabi-sabi bathroom, where it is the point.** **Two deformation mechanisms with rules attached**: general movement (never fix a large flat surface rigidly; **a worked failure where joinery built tight around a washing machine both cannot expand and is shaken loose by vibration**; framed fronts beat solid panels; **shelves near water are a maintenance trap rather than a material failure**, since a worktop wipes easily and a shelf means unloading everything), and **cupping along the growth rings, which bows a single large piece even if well dried — remedied by narrow lamellae, 5–9 cm wide.** **⚠️ The central prescription is oil over lacquer, argued by mechanism: lacquer is a film that protects only while hermetic, and once cracked lets moisture in that cannot evaporate back out — which is where rot starts. Yacht lacquer is no exception and also yellows the timber; enamel scratches; stain needs lacquer over it and shows every chip. Oil fills the pores so there is no room for moisture, lets any that enters evaporate back out, and can be repaired by sanding and re-oiling.** **Wooden basins rejected outright** — "хуже, чем садовые гномики," and uncoated ones last a couple of weeks and go slimy — with the general rule "в плане сантехники лучше не искать приключений, выбирайте керамику." **Barn board carries a specific caution**: often too warped for even a purpose-cut basin recess, and its deep cracks collect dirt that cannot be cleaned out — "если вы покупаете предмет с историей, то вы покупаете и всё, что к этой истории прилагается." **Small wooden items tolerate humidity far better than large ones.** No prices. [[_Sources/YT_B7G_oKHEjkc_kuzina_natural_wood_in_bathroom|note]].

- `yt_Dd_pUAvzqew` — "Идеальная люстра над обеденным столом" (2022-06-01, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 12).** Six minutes, and unusually dense in checkable rules for its length. **⚠️ Diameter formula: table WIDTH — not length — minus 30 cm**, scoped to rectangular tables seating no more than three per long side; **for four or more per side, two fixtures rather than one larger, and the failure mode is named** — a single big many-lamp fixture over a 10–12 seat table dazzles whoever sits opposite it at the middle of the long edge. Round table: diameter minus 30; up to 90 cm diameter a single-bulb pendant from about 40 cm works; a square table is the round case. **⚠️ Height 75–90 cm above the tabletop with ceilings to 3 m — which independently corroborates Шеврина/SMBUREAU's 75–80 cm already in this store from an unrelated channel**, and adds two checks that figure lacked: at a 75 cm table the underside then lands at a standing person's eye line or slightly above, **and the fixture must also be sized against ceiling height, by subtracting table height and the gap and then the canopy and suspension.** The common error she names is hanging a dining pendant at bedroom/living-room height. **Rescue for a blocked view, wallpaper or TV: an open frame or lattice fixture** — which also rescues a fixture hung at the wrong height. **She endorses buying chandeliers that do not really light**, since they are decoration that must look good switched off — **a position the lighting engineer in `9MsEZVjLH2M` independently confirms from the opposite premises.** Formality register as a selection lever: crystal and glossy metal read formal, wood/weave/exposed bulb lower the "градус пафоса." **One worked ceiling-height example is arithmetically inconsistent in the captions and was deliberately not extracted.** No prices. [[_Sources/YT_Dd_pUAvzqew_kuzina_dining_pendant_size_and_height|note]].

- `yt_9MsEZVjLH2M` — "Подкаст: что такое светодизайн. Беседа с основателем @500lux Сергеем Ренжиным" (2022-08-25, Надежда Кузина × Сергей Реньжин) — **FULL (fact_yield 21), `promotional_ratio: medium` — the highest of Round 1**, because the guest plugs his studio's express-project service with an on-air promo code and a running thread is the case for hiring a lighting designer, which is his business. **Extracted in full because his strongest claims cut against that interest**: less light is better than more, recessed fixtures can be bought cheaply, Chinese fixtures are fine, and the visible difference between a good and a mediocre fixture is only 15–20%. **⚠️ Region upgrade: he states he lives in Sochi, relocated from Moscow ~2020 — so 500lux is a level-1 Sochi studio**, upgrading that channel's provisional Group E entry. **⚠️ The central mechanism explains several rules this store already holds as assertions**: a bright unshielded source in the field of view (a bare lamp, no optics, ~350° emission) **makes the eye adapt down so you stop seeing the space** — "ни одна лампочка за тридцать рублей может убить дизайн за несколько миллионов." **⚠️ And it supplies the physiological mechanism behind this store's same-scene colour-temperature rule, which Kruglov stated without one**: seeing different colour temperatures in one room **splits the focus of attention**, so you stop reading it as a single space. **⚠️ "Люстра — не прибор освещения," and the compounding error is that designers build the whole scheme around it — "колоссальная ошибка" — because a chandelier flattens the design: different colours and wall patterns all read the same once uniform light descends. "Люстра — убийца интерьера."** Also: **CRI should be above 95**; the complete set of objective fixture parameters is colour temperature, luminous flux, secondary optics and CRI plus form factor, **so there is no other axis on which a fixture can be claimed to light better** — a usable filter against marketing; tunable white is now baseline having been 3× list a few years earlier; **scenarios deflated** — in a 45 m² flat "это называется выключатель," with a two-scenario baseline (weekday, plus evening/duty for the night trip) and **a bedside strip that must sit in an aluminium profile so the LED does not degrade**; **kids' room illuminance in work zones "все про это забывают"**; functional layer first with a 60° recessed downlight as the named default, accents only where there is something to accent; and **"дизайн первичен, всегда. Свет — это просто макияж."** **⚠️ A tier-steering finding worth keeping**: showrooms cannot serve a real project because their task is to clear stock, and structurally cannot — one downlight exists in 10–15 optics/temperature variants — **and lighting factories capture design studios by offering to draw the lighting plan free, with his claim that 9 of 10 studios never place fixtures themselves.** Read as one competitor's testimony, not established fact. **He also names his own most expensive mistake**: €300-apiece German asymmetric-optic fixtures wall-grazing a wall the client never mentioned would be marble, whose gloss reflected the source and destroyed the effect — generalising to *check every washed surface for gloss before specifying a directional fixture.* **The express project's price is stated but garbled beyond recovery in the captions and was not guessed**; no other price appears. [[_Sources/YT_9MsEZVjLH2M_kuzina_renzhin_lighting_design_podcast|note]].

- `yt_7Kk_TTxoq4g` — «Магия орнамента», seminar at the LeDimore/de Mora showroom (2017-10-16, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 20), `promotional_ratio: medium`** (venue-hosted, examples from the host's collections). **This is the channel's longest video, and it tests Round 1's "format beats topic" finding — the honest answer is: only partly.** Roughly 60% is art history and prehistoric
