symbolism supporting no renovation decision, and **yield per minute is by far the worst of any Кузина source** (20 facts / 92 min against 38 / 78 and 27 / 47). **But the last fifteen minutes carry a scale-hierarchy rule this vault had nowhere.** **⚠️ Combining patterns is a scale problem as much as a colour one** — the boldest ornament should also be the most tonally contrasting **and the largest**, then finer, then finer, down to near-plain surfaces carrying only faint texture. **One dominant eye-catcher, or two separated into different zones, and everything else supporting in defined proportions.** **⚠️ Why a heavily patterned room need not read overloaded**: eight or nine distinct textile and surface variants can stay calm **because they sit close in tonality and there is a large quantity of neutral ground** — "любой ткани нужно более спокойное обрамление." **She rejects the widely-repeated odd-number-of-fabrics convention outright** ("с тканями это вообще никак не работает") while also rejecting one-fabric-everywhere and "more is better" — **the constraint is relationship, not count.** Plus the anchor method (one multi-colour fabric leads, every other contains one of its colours), the rug as binder, and Kelly Wearstler as the case where apparent whimsy is built almost mathematically. **⚠️ Gradient is a proportion tool with a prescriptive direction: dark low, light high raises the ceiling, and below 3 m ceilings "не отступать от этого правила."** Also trellis behaviour (transparent, airy, but geometry will eat it — pair with florals or plain fields), meander as border-not-fill, damask's non-Damascus origin, and **the white stripe as the authenticity marker of real woven ikat.** [[_Sources/YT_7Kk_TTxoq4g_kuzina_ornament_seminar|note]].

- `yt_xeJWHR77QlE` — «Ваби-саби, хюгге, лагом: интерьер со смыслом или просто тренд?», Лемана ПРО podcast (2026-04-20) — **PARTIAL (fact_yield 8), `promotional_ratio: medium-high` — the highest of any Кузина source.** A 49-minute discursive conversation about trends and marketing on **the retailer's own branded podcast**, with an in-content marketplace plug. **Almost no renovation technique, no dimensions, no materials guidance, no prices.** **⚠️ The one item that justifies processing it at all is a structural account of *why* style-philosophies keep appearing**, delivered by Кузина *on a retailer's podcast*, which makes it more credible not less: "философия насаждается теми, кто должен что-нибудь продать." **The mechanism is specific** — pre-social-media brands had a brief window and used compressed emotional slogans; those stopped working, and social media is instead a long daily conversation, **which requires a worldview to talk about.** So brands promote a philosophy and correlate it with the client's, to build loyalty. **A named style-philosophy is a marketing artefact first.** **Жанна Бадальян records the opposite causation** (lifestyle first, marketing follows and exploits until revulsion) — **both agree the pendulum is real and disagree on which end drives it.** **Both dismiss Pantone's colour of the year as pure marketing, not a vector to follow.** Also: **her actual demand observation, "все хотят минимализм," understood differently by each client**; Scandi and Japandi furniture is mid-century 1950s–60s furniture with different upholstery; **wabi-sabi and Japandi are about фактура not colour and depend heavily on resolved lighting design**; and **the best item — asked how to make an interior match your inner world, she declines the premise** ("можно остаться без ремонта") and both designers say the first question is about **образ жизни, not worldview**, which independently reinforces her own verb-list method. [[_Sources/YT_xeJWHR77QlE_kuzina_lemanapro_philosophy_podcast|note]].

- `yt_SC6icQAYFhw` — «Что делать, если у вас низкий потолок» (2020-09-09, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 31). The strongest source of Round 2 by a wide margin**, and one of the densest single-problem sources on the channel — concrete dimensions, explicit anti-patterns, and a mechanism attached to nearly every rule. **⚠️ The problem is largely self-inflicted and she quantifies it**: the highest new-build ceiling she has heard of is 320 cm, **of which ~20 cm goes to screed and plasterboard**, so "потолок неожиданно из 3,20 превращается в 2,90 или меньше." **⚠️ Three ceiling-void figures that decide the lighting strategy: recessed fixtures cost a minimum 8 cm, plasterboard without them ~5 cm, a good stretch ceiling (Clipso) ~3 cm.** **⚠️ White does not make a ceiling read highest — a misconception**; the highest-reading ceiling is painted **the wall colour, half a tone to a tone lighter**, because the ceiling sits in shadow while walls are lit. **Semi-gloss paint yes; a glossy stretch ceiling no ("выглядит просто как пластик") — matte only.** **Glossy floors also raise the perceived ceiling.** **⚠️ The governing principle: the eye must not be able to locate the wall/ceiling line, because that line defines the wall's height** — hence a radius, via cornice or plaster. **⚠️ And the consequence people get wrong: with a radius you cannot paint wall and ceiling different colours**, because that re-establishes the line you erased. **Cornice geometry: more onto the ceiling than onto the wall — low height, large depth — and some cornices can be inverted.** Never below 5 cm; under 10 cm she calls small; **if a proper cornice will not fit, fit none.** **⚠️ Cove lighting is self-defeating and this is a second independent objection to a detail Kruglov already cautions against on dust grounds**: it needs ≥5 cm to the ceiling for light to escape, **so it illuminates exactly the junction you were hiding** — fix by putting a light moulding *under* the cornice so the light washes the cornice. **Drop the chandelier entirely and use technical light**; tracks fit any style including classical; **always specify a diffuser, because strip and corner joints show.** **⚠️ The counter-intuitive furniture rule: what matters is the proportion between furniture height and the wall strip above it, and half-and-half is the worst case** — and **do not use low furniture in a *small* room** (a low ceiling is not a small room; low furniture there reads like cropped trousers). Plus the door-to-ceiling over-panel, **which is mutually exclusive with a ceiling cornice**, and curtain niches with no pelmets. [[_Sources/YT_SC6icQAYFhw_kuzina_low_ceilings|note]].

- `yt_AafdFwF_Rgs` — «10 правил как сочетать деревянные отделки в интерьере» (2021-04-04, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 22).** **⚠️ Lands directly on this store's open tone-count Perspectives block, where Кузина was already a party via secondary mentions — this is her primary statement, and it reframes the disagreement rather than adding a vote.** **The decisive content is a controlled two-step experiment**: darken a partition keeping its yellow undertone → **more varied, less laconic, "но это не хорошо и не плохо"**; change brightness *and* undertone to reddish → **"это уже не разнообразный интерьер, а просто вудхаус," "интерьер разваливается."** **⚠️ Conclusion: vary brightness freely; never vary undertone — the failure everyone attributes to "too many tones" is an undertone collision.** She also **relativises her own rule of three**, presenting a designer working with five to seven finishes as a signature and calling it beautiful — **the count is a heuristic, the structure and undertone discipline are the requirements.** Plus: **significance follows visual area, so not every finish deserves equal effort** (least: chair legs; most: floors, doors, wall panels — **and doors are elevated because they repeat room to room and tie the flat together**); choose a principal finish deliberately rather than letting area choose it; **warm and cold are relative, so if you have cold wood take warmth from the wall colour, not a second wood**; **only one bold texture**; differences must be obvious or the room reads as failed tone-matching; **"лучше использовать одну фактуру в разных цветах, чем один цвет в разных фактурах"** — texture is style-bound where colour is not; **"при сомнении разделяйте"** (a rug between a dark table and light floors; move a wall panel so the wardrobe stands against paint); and **⚠️ the sampling rule that generalises past wood — never choose by photo, and never from memory across two differently-lit shops; take a sample board on deposit, or use a sample-delivery service to view under your own light.** [[_Sources/YT_AafdFwF_Rgs_kuzina_combining_wood_finishes|note]].

- `yt_LAuZhM9tJWA` — «Как сочетать металлические отделки в интерьере» (2020-02-16, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 21). Nothing else in this vault covers metal-finish coordination at all**, and she states outright that the rules are induced from photographs of real interiors **because the information is not in the literature** — so treat them as one practitioner's systematisation. **⚠️ The opening is the most useful part because it shows how the problem actually arises — a procurement cascade, not a design decision**: buy a gold pendant before choosing the kitchen → gold-profile facades to match → no gold in standard hardware, so buy gold handles → **but the built-in appliances have silver handles, and gold-handled built-ins are very hard to source**, so settle for black → then a mixer roughly matching. **Assembled, the "gold" pendant reads almost silver in daylight and nothing matches.** **And the obvious fix — chrome everything, leave the pendant — reads as a random set.** **⚠️ Hierarchy is the answer, with figures: the dominant metal takes ~70%, and her contrast pair proves the threshold** — a bathroom with only two metals at ~60/40 **still fails because near-half-and-half has no dominant**, against a success at 70–80% in one family with a rose-gold accent at 20–30%. **⚠️ Two metals in a small room, up to four in a large one — the reason is object count**, since a small room has too few objects for each not to carry its own finish. **⚠️ Metals combine when they are *different*: warm + cold, glossy + matte, or anything + black** ("чёрный сочетается с любым металлом"). **Two *similar* colours fail — "просто как ошибка" — and worst in cold metals: silver+nickel, silver+chrome, chrome+nickel will fail if they share a surface treatment**, which is why two chromes clash. **⚠️ Naming is unreliable between suppliers**: a maker with brass but no gold will label the brass "gold," and brass is often sold as "champagne." Plus **the mix includes anything with metallic sheen** — textiles, sheen wallpaper, rust-imitating tile — and the distribution rules (spread the dominant evenly; distribute by height, a rule she doubts; **and give objects in different finishes something in common — a chandelier containing several finishes ties gold and silver side tables together**). [[_Sources/YT_LAuZhM9tJWA_kuzina_combining_metal_finishes|note]].

- `yt_0pqfj2JdAGU` — «Как использовать дверь в интерьере» (2022-04-28, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 17); roughly the first 40% is a historical preamble on prehistoric doors and was deliberately not extracted.** **⚠️ Two theses: on a small budget choose neutral doors *or* make an art object of them, nothing in between; and a door never plays the leading role** — the reason being physical rather than aesthetic, that **the door is behind you once you have entered.** "Если вы усложняете дверь — усложняйте и сам интерьер." Her analogies set the ceiling on ambition: **the background landscape in a Renaissance painting**, and **theatre scenery that does not replace the actors.** **⚠️ The strongest practical argument: a decorated door forces matching commitments across the whole flat.** Diagonal veneer plus routed slots needs diagonal parquet or supporting wall battens; **a door with hexagons is "самый трэш"** because you must repeat hexagons in every room or accept different doors on each side of the opening. **⚠️ The edge-colour procedure, easy to apply: stand in the room and pull the door toward you — the colour that then appears is the outside face's colour, and that is the edge colour.** Plus: **доборы and architrave edges can be a contrasting colour unrelated to the leaf** (her black доборы running into a black skirting give "тонкую графику" without much black); **treat a door as part of the wall to hide a badly placed one**; **in symmetrical arrangements one of the pair is often a false door**; bi-folds where an open leaf would obstruct a room's shape; and **doors need not all match — her own hallway has three, with three architrave treatments — but hierarchy must be logical** (big sliding glass to a bedroom while the living room gets a small ordinary door is a worse error than mixed architraves in one room). [[_Sources/YT_0pqfj2JdAGU_kuzina_door_as_design_element|note]].

- `yt_VBMzas01VRs` — «Квартира неправильной формы: как исправить геометрию» (2021-02-13, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 18). The predicted second source for the irregular-geometry gap — but both are Кузина, so the pair stays `single-account` and the page threshold is still not met.** **⚠️ Two concrete costs rather than an aesthetic complaint**: the central part of such a flat cannot really be used, serving only as circulation; and **without right angles, bulky furniture can go only in the centre or along one wall.** **⚠️ A pre-purchase checklist against the developer's own plan** — rounded, obtuse or acute angles, niches, projections, beams, non-standard openings, any non-rectangular room — **and a commercial warning: when a salesperson calls the asymmetry a feature, "не надо на это вестись." A flat with skewed angles is not cheaper while losing more functional area.** **⚠️ The governing move: straighten the *habitable* volumes at the expense of circulation and storage — "лучше иметь кривой коридор, чем кривую спальню."** **Combining rooms is the strongest wall-level fix** (the larger the room, the less the defect reads), **and the consequent furniture rule is to pull furniture off the walls and group it by function.** Plus **the two-islands technique with paired items to make an invented axis legible**; orienting to a chamfer **only if it exceeds ~30 cm**; **non-standard furniture is unavoidable under ~20 m² because any bulky right-angled object emphasises the shape**; **⚠️ and the cost lever — a wardrobe divided into regular 60–65 cm modules that turn with the wall, with a single non-standard corner element joining the runs.** Also the counter-intuitive option of **making it *more* irregular** ("как спрятать иголку в стоге сена"), and two distinct colour strategies — bright colour to distract, or painting the problem zone in a shade continuing onto other zones to dissolve it. **Her verdict: in most cases a disadvantage, not an advantage, and it entails additional expenditure.** [[_Sources/YT_VBMzas01VRs_kuzina_irregular_apartment_geometry|note]].

- `yt_E6nYBdz9NhM` — «Дизайн маленькой квартиры. 10 советов в дополнение к тем, что можно встретить на Ютубе» (2022-02-05, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 24), and it is a *rebuttal* source: she lists the standard small-flat advice and then attacks the parts that have no mechanism.** Her method shows in the smallest example — "я не поняла, почему именно в маленьком помещении шторы не должны валяться на полу, а в среднем помещении могут." **Her summary objection: follow all of it and you get interiors "под копирку."** **⚠️ Opens a genuine Perspectives disagreement with Шеврина/SMBUREAU (`9h2tAnm6rqA`)**, recorded on `Color_Palette_and_Material_Direction.md`: they agree on a restrained low-contrast **envelope** and split on **accents.** **Кузина explicitly rejects "keep everything calm so the eye glides"** — "это всё равно что скакать на лошади, отпустив поводья… глазом и умом зрителя нужно управлять" — and wants an accent sited toward the **centre** of the room to pull attention off a low ceiling. **Her reconciling move may narrow the gap: the accent need not be large or bright, only interesting.** **⚠️ A separate correction: a monochrome palette does not have to be light** — "монохромная не означает чёрно-белое, это означает, что цвет должен быть один." **⚠️ And the mechanism the received advice never states — "работа с коробкой": the brain judges area by finding where the planes end, so blur the boundaries.** Hence walls and ceiling one colour, no accent wall, rugs merging into the floor; **a large door-less opening to the next room so the eye stops hitting a wall** (transformative for an 8–10 m² bedroom) — **with two scoped exceptions, an untidy kitchen and specifically the room-plus-hallway pairing**; transoms and walls stopping short of the ceiling; **mirrors pressed into a corner** — **with two honest cautions the standard advice omits: mirrors and transparent furniture both cause real discomfort in a small room, because you never quite command the space.** Plus **furniture detached from both floor and ceiling as a two-plane zoning device**, the storage cost of a floating wardrobe, and multifunction furniture. [[_Sources/YT_E6nYBdz9NhM_kuzina_small_apartment_rebuttal|note]].

- `yt_X3emRuW-PbA` — «Какая плитка и керамогранит подходят под какой стиль» (2023-03-16, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 24).** Scoped explicitly to **appearance, not technical characteristics**; «плитка» covers ceramic and porcelain throughout. **⚠️ The item she flags as her own blind spot: tile designs imitate *specific named* marbles, not "marble" generically** — Statuario (the most expensive Italian white; contrasting, sparse veins), Calacatta (more diffuse, more veins, same Carrara quarry), Arabescato (more contrasting, veins enclosing rounded areas — "a grey ocean seen from above"), Emperador, Sahara Noir. **⚠️ Naming hazard: her preferred Invisible Grey is sold as Invisible White, Invisible Pearl and "Invisible-something" depending on the maker — the trade name does not identify the stone.** Three style groups: **strict neoclassic** (marble-effect default; ⚠️ never two marbles at the same pattern scale; ⚠️ never marble with onyx/agate — best case a saturated luxury that reads Art Deco, i.e. you land in a different style; wood-effect floors in dark/mid tones only, ⚠️ tested by **«много лиц»** — you must not see where the pattern repeats; chequerboard success is the chequer-to-room ratio; octagon is English/Victorian; ⚠️ herringbone/chevron/hexagon get their interest from the *laying* pattern, not the tile; ⚠️ floor borders easy to overdo); **country/farmhouse/Provence/shabby chic** (⚠️ separated from neoclassic by texture and ornament with less contrast and gloss — "про пафос неправда, просто он другой"; simpler wood species, floor only; non-noble stone; aged and factory-made-to-look-handmade tile with deliberately widened joints; terracotta; ⚠️ **zellige identified by mechanism** — hand-dipped glaze fired at deliberately uneven temperature, so no two tiles match, which is what separates it from serial imitation); **contemporary/modernism** (⚠️ large monolithic surfaces are *why* plaster and paint beat wallpaper; ⚠️ large-format wood-effect porcelain as a real, cheaper alternative to timber wall panels — no warping, free cutting, pre-drawn predictable grain; metro tile from the early-20th-century New York subway, ⚠️ **and with a bevel it is «кабанчик», which suits only classic or retro**; terrazzo, ⚠️ with the trick of taking a chip colour as the room's paint colour; ⚠️ concrete-effect needs refined partners or you land in Loft). **⚠️ She then calls the whole three-group division approximate and names the rule that outranks it: tile must relate to every room in the home, including rooms it is not laid in — and if the flat mixes, commit to eclecticism properly rather than mislabelling it neoclassic.** [[_Sources/YT_X3emRuW-PbA_kuzina_tile_types_by_style|note]].

- `yt_zrnhNEgk-Sk` — «Дизайн интерьера своими руками» (2023-04-20, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 26), and ⚠️ the most directly actionable source from this channel: a checklist, not a principle.** She reverse-engineered why Russian design portfolios converge and gave the recipe away, deadpan — *"это не означает, что все дизайнеры дураки."* **⚠️ Carries the only design-fee benchmark in this vault: Moscow, April 2023, flat up to 100 m² — a designer from 200,000 RUB (~$3,100), a design course 150,000–250,000 RUB (~$2,300–$3,800) plus a lot of your time. The comparison is the durable content: hiring and learning cost the same order of magnitude.** **⚠️ Governing claim: "в типовом проекте стиля как такового нет"** — only slightly-more-classical or slightly-more-contemporary, with **identical treatment for living room, kitchen and bedroom.** Palette: cream/light-grey/light-beige fronts, greyish-beige velvet upholstery, **living-room walls cream and only cream**, plain rugs with **ornament forbidden**, **the dining chairs as the single bright object**, flat white ceilings with **no level changes.** **⚠️ The empty-wall method is the reusable core**: moulding rectangles («зеркала») wide-in-the-centre with narrow flanks, picture in the wide one and wall lights in the narrow ones, all painted one colour — or, contemporary, asymmetric mouldings / floor-to-ceiling slats at a third or half the sofa width; frescoes permitted but **no bright colours and no pronounced subject**; TV wall treated identically. Lighting reduces to **balls, sticks, balls-on-sticks and rings** — usable as a negative specification. Kitchen: **«кухня-рамка»** linear run between two columns, all fronts matte/plain/solid, **cylindrical extractor only**, and **one rule setting five metal finishes at once (classical → gold, contemporary → black).** Living room: **the Lawson sofa in every project**, style carried entirely by the arms, **only two layouts.** **⚠️ A budgeting aside worth its own line: on a render, laminate, quartz-vinyl and parquet are near-indistinguishable — the visualisation cannot justify a flooring upgrade.** Closes honestly: **"типовой проект не значит плохой"** — the designer is serving the request the majority has. [[_Sources/YT_zrnhNEgk-Sk_kuzina_typical_project_recipe|note]].

- `yt_gwa1wsTYYLE` — «Как сделать комнату светлее» (2022-10-06, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 20). Structurally the twin of `E6nYBdz9NhM`**: she delivers the standard advice competently, marks where it ends — *"на этом заканчиваются советы, которые можно найти в сети"* — and asks whether following it produces an interior anyone wants. The conventional half: **glass partitions that need not run floor to ceiling (a partial one reads as an internal window)**; **glass blocks for hallways and corridors, which most often have no daylight at all**; **⚠️ light-transmitting textile instead of glass, because light interiors read like a hospital and textile is what removes that**; white walls as the best choice for a dark room (conceded against her own taste); textured and concrete-effect plaster absorbing light; **⚠️ fewer shadows means a lighter interior — open-back chairs, furniture on high legs, and rounded corners** (independently the same move `fSBAMSIgnBs` recommends for wear); mirrored or glossy window reveals; light floors; decluttered windowsills; **⚠️ rooms with a balcony are the darkest, because the balcony holds everything that did not fit in the flat**; unlined translucent curtains paired with roller blinds — **⚠️ and she flags without resolving that this contradicts her own zoning advice: "да, такая я противоречивая."** **⚠️ Then the pivot: follow every recommendation seriously and you get the interior Russians commonly end up with anyway — creamy beige, no decor, no ornament, light floors, mirrors opposite the windows.** Her question is left genuinely open; **the rule she does commit to is "недостаток света — это недостаток, но это не конец света" — defects should be corrected, but not at the expense of other parameters.** **⚠️ Her way out and its cost: the two main design parameters are texture and form, so when light and dark are unavailable only those remain — and "синяя краска или белая краска — цена примерно одинаковая, но скульптурный стульчик и обычный стульчик — цена скорее всего будет разная." Colour decisions are near cost-neutral; form decisions are not.** Plus a glass-partition reality check given immediately after recommending them: **expensive mechanisms, glass blocks impose a style, loft partitions read banal, and ⚠️ you cannot stand furniture along a glass partition — so it worsens the storage problem it was not meant to touch.** [[_Sources/YT_gwa1wsTYYLE_kuzina_natural_light_rebuttal|note]].

- `yt_Oy_eTelj-X0` — «Как организовать рабочее место дома» (2020-04-09, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 16), `promotional_ratio: none`.** Scoped to a work area *inside* a bedroom or living room, not a separate study; April 2020, the first weeks of pandemic home-working. **⚠️ Carries the most load-bearing definition to reach the zoning page: putting a desk in a bedroom is not a work zone, it is a change of layout.** A zone exists only when the *space* is treated — **a dropped ceiling, dedicated fittings above it, a fragment of screening wall, or a different wall material.** Prompted by clients wanting a **13 m²** children's room zoned four ways: *"и так не бывает."* **⚠️ The consequence: any plan labelling zones should be audited against this test — if only furniture placement changes, the zone is notional.** Separation is **not** always needed: not for a calm temperament, and **⚠️ not when the work uses different faculties than speech** (her example is handwork) — a criterion based on the task, not the room. It **is** needed for negotiation, video calls, high concentration, and for people who cannot get into work mode at home. **⚠️ Whoever sits in the through-route gets interrupted** — her own lived case, and the passer-by sees your screen, which is the other half of it. **⚠️ A substantial rebuttal of "put the desk at the window"**: in many standard plans the window faces the door so entrants see your back and your screen; sun in your eyes or on the monitor; **the radiator under the window heats you separately from the rest of your body** — **fix: sit side-on, or use a corner desk.** **⚠️ Open-through shelving passes light but blocks no sound at all** — light-passing separation is never acoustic separation. Plus phased setup (desk now, storage planned), the one-room tactic of partitioning off part of the *table*, psychological anchors and rituals, and **⚠️ a negative result reported against the standard advice: pinboards of reminders behind the monitor did not help her at all** — decorate over the desk instead, because you are in a living room, not an office. [[_Sources/YT_Oy_eTelj-X0_kuzina_home_workspace|note]].

- `yt_dIdcLPKj6is` — «Спа дома: как сделать ванную комнату местом отдыха» (2023-10-26, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 29), the highest-yield source of Round 3.** **⚠️ Its load-bearing claim is what separates it from generic content: spa advice is superficial, and applying one or two items produces no effect at all — most of the recommendations must be executed together.** Organised as three groups usable as a specification structure: **tactile comfort, sound and smell, visual comfort.** Historical framing (Greek baths, Roman thermae) yields the design consequence: **what survives is an emphasis on material and the assumption of a long stay — and a bathroom programmed for a long stay is a different room.** **⚠️ Hard gate: barefoot use of porcelain or stone requires a heated floor — cold floor and "вы не в спа, а просто в санузле."** **⚠️ The transferable technique is tactile contrast including on surfaces nobody touches** — the perceived smoothness of the bath comes from the roughness around it; pebble surfacing *behind* the bath rather than underfoot is the sharpest example. **⚠️ A steam-room table where humidity, temperature and ventilation obligation line up**: infrared cabin (no steam, ≤60 °C, safest), Finnish sauna (≤15 %, ≤100 °C, natural ventilation may suffice), hammam (100 %, 45–65 °C, **forced ventilation and natural supply mandatory**) — **the higher the humidity, the bigger the ventilation problem, and the failure mode is smell, fungus or mould.** Cedar valued for releasing essential oils on heating; **timber unsuitable for a hammam.** **⚠️ A Russian regulatory block — factory-assembled units only, no collective-use saunas or wood-fired stoves, brick/monolithic buildings with RC slabs only, ≤2 m² taken from bathroom or corridor, housing/fire/sanitary approvals with the set differing by city — recorded on the bathroom page with the jurisdiction flagged, NOT in `16_Legal_and_Regulations/`, which is Belarus-only.** Fittings ladder from whirlpool baths (**⚠️ must be used weekly or the system degrades**) down to a multi-mode shower head (**many heads mix water with air, and differently**) and a foot bath. **⚠️ Dim-to-warm named as the specific lighting feature to specify**, plus a finish constraint — **if RGB is planned, specify cream walls and grey floors, because some finishes look very bad under coloured light.** **⚠️ And she rebuts the standard styling advice: open shelves of scrubs and stacked towels work in a salon because staff re-fold them all day** — leave out only what is needed every five minutes. Closes on the actual design move: **"вы повышаете статус ванны от обыкновенного санузла до комнаты."** [[_Sources/YT_dIdcLPKj6is_kuzina_home_spa_bathroom|note]].

- `yt_bVmE50y33Ls` — «Что взять с собой из старой квартиры» (2020-08-21, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 22), `promotional_ratio: none`. ⚠️ A budget source disguised as a decor source**: renovation is expensive, so the temptation is to move as much as possible — **every item carried across is furniture not purchased, and every wrong one is a design constraint accepted for free.** **⚠️ Her three-question method, in ascending order of validity**: emotional attachment (**test: put it out of sight; if a week later you have not remembered it, discard it**), **sunk cost** — her live case of two identical Italian pendants that do not suit the new flat but were expensive, which she calls *"повод для размышлений," not a reason* — and the only one she endorses, **that it genuinely fits, including by size.** **⚠️ The de-anchoring test: imagine seeing the object for the first time, not knowing who gave it to you — it was not given to you at all.** **⚠️ The mechanism behind souvenir failure: many small disparate objects do not form a rhythm, they form noise — "you merely resign yourself to their existing where you put them" — whereas many *homogeneous* objects work.** Conversation pieces excepted (the Philippe Starck squeezer as the type). **⚠️ Pictures are the category she calls a disaster** — do not bring what does not suit the style unless committing to eclecticism from the start, what suited an interior you are demolishing, or **the largest category, pictures that suit nothing anywhere**, which clients disproportionately own and arrive wanting to build the scheme around; avoid staggered series. **Do bring abstract work, ≥1 m on a side, in a good frame — the frame ties an ordinary picture to the interior and makes it read as more expensive, and it is a separate cost line.** **⚠️ Never reproductions of famous paintings — "it is the same phenomenon as the bears in the pine forest, only now it is an abstract woman and it is a Matisse."** **⚠️ The proportionality rule, the most transferable line: the constraint an object imposes on the plan should be proportional to its value** — a **2,000 RUB (~$30)** reproduction dictating which pier gets the cabinet fails it, while **rare posters, etchings or engravings at ~10,000 RUB (~$150) plus framing** pass, because *"эстетическая ценность больше, чем его денежная стоимость"* — and they give the home the look of having been collected rather than decorated in ten minutes. **⚠️ Plus a directly adoptable procedure: textile samples are always tried at the client's, never judged in the salon**, because the same fabric reads differently under different light and at different room sizes. *(Both RUB figures are her illustrative examples, not quotes; the ~5× ratio is the durable content. Moscow, August 2020.)* [[_Sources/YT_bVmE50y33Ls_kuzina_what_to_bring_from_old_flat|note]].

- `yt_H43m5bn7W8k` — «Почему 3D визуализация — это не дизайн-проект» (2019-10-31, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 14). The oldest source processed from this channel**, prompted by two recent clients insisting they wanted a visualisation rather than a design project. **⚠️ She argues against her own most saleable deliverable, which is what makes it credible.** The chain that must precede a render: **measurements** (*"клиентов, которые в состоянии сами снять досконально замеры своей территории, меньшинство"*; hireable standalone) → **layout** (**⚠️ a render without one is pointless — the image presupposes where the sofa stands and where guests take their shoes off**) → **styling**, which establishes style, colour **and which finishes fit the budget.** **⚠️ The budget rule and the load-bearing claim: "мы работаем над проектом не с целью нарисовать визуализацию, а с целью его воплотить" — so the cost of implementation must be visible from the very first stages, and a concept developed without that number attached may not be buildable.** Going direct to a visualiser means specifying exhaustively yourself — **⚠️ which assumes a stability she says does not hold, because even clients certain of their vision change it during the process.** **⚠️ Practically no designer gets a usable visualisation first pass** (checked with her own visualiser and colleagues), so **you must make the corrections yourself, and that needs a trained eye — otherwise the first output is also the final one.** **⚠️ Her analogy: a designer is an *interface*, like Windows showing windows and buttons instead of raw binary; a visualiser takes a brief and returns "task completed," and nothing else happens.** Then **⚠️ working drawings, which are the actual builder instructions and legitimately correct the render's dimensions — concept and colours hold, furniture or room dimensions may shift, so a render is not a contract.** **⚠️ And the concession: "сказать, что 3D визуализация — это непременный атрибут того, что у вас будет происходить на объекте, нельзя"** — projects without 3D exist, an experienced designer's **collage suffices**, and the honest trade is that **the nuances a correction pass would have caught surface on site instead — the most expensive place to find them.** [[_Sources/YT_H43m5bn7W8k_kuzina_render_is_not_a_design_project|note]].

- `yt_GEBJkzYd874` — «Что не так с правилом 60/30/10» (2022-03-17, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 24), `promotional_ratio: none`. ⚠️ The first source in this vault to argue AGAINST a rule the vault already records as *shared* — the 60/30/10 formula, independently stated by several sources on the curtain pages.** She first states it more operationally than the vault held it (**60% = walls or large objects, often neutral; 30% = smaller furniture, textile, painted doors; 10% = decor and mouldings; one colour at several lightnesses counts as ONE colour; to add a fourth colour keep the ratio and add the accent also at 10%, "как бы 110"**), then attacks it: **interiors with any number of colours succeed; the count depends on style (maximalism needs more than three); and "what counts as a colour?" is never answered** — on one reading there are only three primaries, and **"нейтральные то считаются, то не считаются за цвет — в зависимости от того, набирается у автора три цвета или нет."** **⚠️ Her demolition is empirical: she counts what is actually in the images used to demonstrate the rule** — white ceilings, white shutters, gold fittings, golden parquet, marble, black window framing — and concludes **"совершенно непонятно, как можно считать пропорцию в таком помещении."** It cannot be checked, so it cannot be wrong. **It also fails outright where a pattern occupies real area.** **What people actually do is «нейтральная база плюс один цвет» — which she says produces the most banal result imaginable.** **⚠️ She then supplies a replacement: Itten's lightness-to-area principle via Goethe's values (yellow 9, violet 3, green 6, red 6), with saturation interacting with area — BUT HER TWO WORKED EXAMPLES INVERT THE STANDARD FORMULATION** (she says three parts yellow to one of violet; Itten's harmonious areas are inversely proportional to light values, so it is three yellow to nine violet). **Flagged in the note and on the wiki page as `verify before use`; the principle is sound, the direction is not.** Closes with the Ильяхов «7,2 преимущества» analogy — **think about meaning first and the formula second** — which generalises to every numeric rule in this vault. [[_Sources/YT_GEBJkzYd874_kuzina_60_30_10_rule_rebuttal|note]].

- `yt_ocoPRS3d8sA` — «Ошибки в работе с цветом» (2022-04-13, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 34) — the highest single-source yield from this channel, and at 31 min the longest source processed from it.** `promotional_ratio: none`. **⚠️ Method note: she controls the variable, deliberately choosing interiors that use the SAME colours (green and pink) throughout so the comparisons isolate distribution rather than hue** — and states she is showing *so-so* interiors rather than bad ones, since bad ones need no explanation. **⚠️ The centrepiece is the BANK HIERARCHY, the most complete answer this vault holds to "how much of each colour": board of directors → top managers → heads of departments → rank-and-file, where more area and more brightness both pull attention.** Four named failure modes (**the whole bank is the board; board plus a few staff with nothing between, so the range is not stretched; board plus top managers only; two tiers spread evenly, which is harmonious but boring**) and a tier-by-tier success reading. **⚠️ Its extension — the outside contractor — allows episodic swappable colours but constrains them: "приглашённый сотрудник не должен соревноваться за власть с советом директоров," and banks do not hire off the street.** **⚠️ And the proportion rule that is its actual answer to 60/30/10: there are fewer bosses than staff, so DO NOT divide the areas equally.** Sixteen mistakes in all, including: **rules are not a panacea** ("хороший интерьер — это тот, который нравится хозяину"); **the problem is distribution, not colour count**; **⚠️ choose furniture, upholstery, finishes and rugs FIRST and wall paint LAST, because "спектр вариантов краски всегда будет больше, чем любой набор обивок"**; **⚠️ an unsupported colour zone is worse than no colour — "лучше бы он просто был бежевым"**; **the A3 sample trap and the buy-it-less-bright fix**; **never choose colour from memory (with a live four-option test on the viewer) and never by its name ("им надо придумать по 50 названий в день")**; **⚠️ the MUSICAL metaphor for whole-flat coherence — you need not repeat one colour or even one palette, only share one saturation or one warmth**; and **⚠️ ignoring style, which she says appears on no online list: swap a style's native palette and the style may stop reading.** Also: dark colours defended, the beige-to-beige counter-caution, and colour reflexes on the face where makeup is applied. [[_Sources/YT_ocoPRS3d8sA_kuzina_colour_mistakes|note]].

- `yt_yU2zKPpDghM` — «Сложности в работе с цветом» (2022-05-05, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 30), `promotional_ratio: none`. The explicit companion to `ocoPRS3d8sA`: that one covered mistakes, this one covers the nuances where there is no right answer.** **⚠️ «Поддержка цвета» — support a loud object with accents of the same colour elsewhere; and the second rule inside the same example, that a large colour patch settles better when broken up by small details of another colour.** **⚠️ But the more valuable half is the counter-move: an object can be left deliberately unsupported** — a large green sofa with no other green is "самодостаточен," and **artworks are often left unsupported precisely to draw attention**; the same designer does both in one project. **Her analogy is the best-remembered thing in the round: it is the absence of a rhyme where you expect one — "розы, морозы, мимозы… такие розы" and then nothing — which wakes the viewer.** **⚠️ Contrast: let one colour be saturated and darken or mute the other; contrast makes an interior more interesting and more tiring.** **⚠️ Single-colour interiors, with four historical worked examples (David Hicks; Diana Vreeland's «Garden of Hell» by Billy Baldwin, where one *ornament* repeats, not just one colour; Mark Hampton; one restaurant where her eye cannot find a focal point) — and the rule for doing it properly: do NOT vary one hue by adding white down a fan-deck column; move horizontally, mixing two different shades of the same family.** **⚠️ Warm/cold is a relative quality; tertiaries cannot be assigned absolutely; adding white makes a colour colder and adding black makes it slightly warmer; green is borderline.** **⚠️ THE GOVERNING RULE: colours must share something — one lightness, OR one saturation, OR one warmth — and the condition for mixing warm and cold is a shared LIGHTNESS, verified by converting a photo to greyscale.** Plus: **neutral does not mean beige** and can be built from very muted versions of the scheme's own accents; **all white interior paints are tinted, so "white" is not a specification**, and different whites read only side by side; **and the ceiling as the fifth wall**, with height effects in both directions and an unplanned-reflex caution. [[_Sources/YT_yU2zKPpDghM_kuzina_colour_difficulties|note]].

- `yt_pFCVO7AuSMw` — «Как выбрать краску для стен» (2022-02-17, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 40) — the highest yield in Round 4 and the most executable source from this channel: a complete working procedure rather than a set of principles.** `promotional_ratio: low`. **⚠️ Her opening observation explains a gap most people notice without diagnosing: salespeople never recommend a colour in the abstract, "потому что если вам вдруг потом не понравится этот цвет на стене, то им будут выслушивать ваши претензии" — so they talk about opacity, consumption and storage instead.** **⚠️ Counter-intuitive core rule: choose a supplier with FEWER colours, because the eye tires of colour as other senses tire under load (her perfume analogy).** Her working catalogues run 156 and ~160 colours and she calls that ample. **⚠️ The scheduling consequence: look at colours for ALL rooms in one session, shortlist, then come back the NEXT DAY and look again.** **⚠️ Order THREE shades of every colour — the chosen one plus one step lighter and one darker — because a set pins the choice down where a single sample cannot.** **⚠️ The tester procedure: minimum A3, painted on hardboard or plasterboard, NEVER on the wall (they must be mobile), with no white paper or green plasterboard margin showing; then held at ceiling level, on the ceiling if the paint runs there (it reads much darker), at eye level, at floor level against the already-laid floor, on the wall opposite the window, on side walls, and in shadow — where a near-white's undertone shows most.** **⚠️ "Ignoring accidental context" as a named skill, with the best explanation in the round: Photoshop uses a grey background specifically so it does not interfere with perception; a grey among lilacs reads pinkish and may not be; and on site the white gypsum filler makes neutrals look far dirtier than they will finish.** **⚠️ Lighting: "даже один цвет в разном освещении может дать гораздо больше разницы в тоне, чем два соседних цвета" — test in the actual room, at different times of day, and against the builder's-bulb problem use a variable-temperature LED panel set to the intended kelvins.** Plus the technical specs to check — **opacity, texture, washability, LRV, fade resistance, adhesion (expensive paint is more flexible, so settlement cracks show later), composition** — **light paint being more practical because the substrate beneath is light**, and **the gloss ladder: ~5% default matte, ~15% eggshell for a balcony or tambour (more latex, more washable), 80–90% only for Art Deco or a 60s–70s revival — with the trap that dark and light paints from the same range differ in gloss despite the same label.** [[_Sources/YT_pFCVO7AuSMw_kuzina_choosing_wall_paint|note]].

- `yt_I-F2ULBDc5A` — «В какой цвет красить плинтусы, молдинги и наличники» (2021-08-10, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 28), `promotional_ratio: low`. ⚠️ Evidence quality above this round's average: several examples are her OWN completed projects described operationally (which element got which colour and why) rather than found images.** **⚠️ It also stands against content already in this vault** — Шеврина/SMBUREAU's recorded case against trims — **and the two are now a Perspectives block on `Doors_Trim_Cost_and_Buying.md`. They agree on less than it appears** (hers is about what to do once trim exists; Шеврина's is a construction argument about junctions) **but there is one head-on collision: Шеврина says remove mouldings where the room has no other classical elements, and Кузина says moulding on concrete is a legitimate classic-plus-loft mix if carried through.** **⚠️ Terminology finding: Russian has no single word for these elements; English "trim" covers them, and also braid on a dress or coloured trims on a car — "и за это тоже нужно заплатить," attention to detail that individualises the thing.** **A three-group taxonomy**: **(1) the colour of an adjoining plane** — cornice and skirting in the wall colour **visually increase wall height** and push the moulding into the background; **white on white works specifically when the moulding is abundant and finely detailed, because detail reads heavy and white lightens it**; **(2) neutrals** — **the greater the lightness difference the greater the accent**; **white edging softens bright walls by breaking the wall up and reducing its area, but visually LOWERS ceilings**; **white as a frame where another room shows through an opening**; **⚠️ white is what stops subtle light shades reading as merely colourless**; **black adds drama, pulls attention off over-active wallpaper, frames openings, and "обнимает" a room with scattered elements**; **wood trim, with a maximum of three wood-effect finishes and a colour slightly offset from the floors**; **(3) another colour** — **never one element alone, use two or preferably three, or support the colour in accessories** — **⚠️ and a cross-room idea: repeat a colour from another room in the trim here (a blue sofa in the living room, blue skirting in the corridor).** **⚠️ Two lifehacks that dodge the decision: paint almost the wall colour, one or two tones off; or keep the colour and change the sheen — super-matte walls with eggshell trim.** [[_Sources/YT_I-F2ULBDc5A_kuzina_trim_and_moulding_colour|note]].

- `yt_lptrnXyf0as` — «Два цвета на одной стене» (2020-01-20, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 17), `promotional_ratio: none`. The shortest source in the round and the only one carrying real dimensions.** **⚠️ The objection she starts from is cultural — schools and hospitals painted the lower wall green — and her reframe is the interesting move: private homes increasingly borrow bar and restaurant devices, so read the same wall as "мы сидим за барной стойкой" and it changes register entirely.** **⚠️ Divide one third to two thirds, justified by the golden section** (hedged as not the only ideal). **⚠️ The collision rules are load-bearing: the boundary must NOT coincide with the door head — with a 2100 door run it at roughly 1800 from finished floor — nor with low furniture along the wall.** **⚠️ Bathroom case with figures: tile below and paint above, the boundary must miss the basin — an 800 basin plus ~200 of splashback puts it at about 1000 — and where the division carries a depth change or moulding, that detail must land UNDER the mirror.** **⚠️ Her concrete statement of what the drawing stage buys: "именно для этого вы нанимаете дизайнера, который может на развёртке перепроверить пропорции."** **⚠️ The furniture trick: where tall furniture crosses the boundary, paint it in the two colours and dissolve it into the wall plane.** Three colour schemes (**darker below grounds the room; two colours of the same lightness, checkable by photographing it grey; or one colour split light and dark**); **⚠️ a short wall divides more harmoniously than a long one — 3 m ceilings with a 5 m wall is a named bad case**; and materials beyond paint, **with the edge («кромка») to resolve on tile and panels and a moulding required between wallpaper and paint so the cut edge is hidden and cannot peel.** [[_Sources/YT_lptrnXyf0as_kuzina_two_colours_one_wall|note]].

- `yt_IBecn-3_7Ls` — «Палитра земли» (2026-08-06, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 14). ⚠️ `promotional_ratio: medium` — the highest of any source from this channel, and the format has visibly changed: scripted, tightly edited, a formal self-introduction, and the Telegram channel plugged twice in six minutes with a QR code, with part of the pigment history explicitly withheld to it.** **⚠️ By far the most recent upload processed from this channel (previous newest 2023-10-26) — recorded as a channel-level observation: the format has professionalised and the promotional load has risen with it.** The direct sequel to the already-processed `n5ZBqdq0wH8`. **⚠️ Her challenge: who decided these are nature's colours? "Где васильки, где море, где северное сияние?"** **⚠️ The answer, and the formulation worth keeping: "палитра земли — это палитра ландшафтов с дефицитом воды. Чем меньше воды в ландшафте, тем чаще мы видим саму землю."** The geography that produces it — south-west USA, Morocco, Algeria, Egypt, parts of Spain and Greece — is different cultures with one landscape type. **Pigment history: these predate any trend, being materials you could pick up and grind; ochre is pigmented clay with hematite, which neither washes out nor decomposes — "цвета руки, держащей кусок земли."** **⚠️ And the part with direct bearing on THIS project: transposed to a northern, damp, grey-light climate the literal palette stops working — with the caveat that forecloses the obvious rebuttal, that a burnt landscape alone is not enough, since "никто не мечтает оформить квартиру в эстетике торфяного пожара."** **Her northern version, built from Levitan (light passing THROUGH cloud rather than falling directly) and Apollinary Vasnetsov (damp, forested, full of half-shadow): more moss, more wood tones, more grey-green and grey-blue.** **⚠️ Conclusion: "никакой единственной природной палитры не существует" — the question is not whether the earth palette suits you but which nature you want to bring home.** **Flagged on the wiki page as a real consideration for this Belarus apartment, which sits in her northern band rather than the Mediterranean one the trend formed in.** [[_Sources/YT_IBecn-3_7Ls_kuzina_earth_palette_northern_version|note]].

- `yt_ubB1GSFtN20` — «Если вы боитесь цвета» (2020-12-20, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 21), `promotional_ratio: none`. ⚠️ The one source in the round addressing the client-side blocker rather than the technique — and the blocker is a COST fear, which makes it a budget source as much as a colour one**: *"эксперименты — это дорого, поэтому если мы промахнёмся с цветом, потом придётся всё переделывать. Ну нафиг, сделаем всё нейтрально"* — **while the same people do not want a faceless hotel interior.** **⚠️ A low-commitment ladder: neutral ground with bright accents (cushions, coloured piping, cords, tassels, tiebacks, lampshades) — but several such objects, never one; then colour dosed into details (window reveals, cabinet END PANELS, bright chair legs, bright drawer interiors or handles); then coloured cornice and skirting; then «скрытый цвет» — colour behind cabinet doors, inside drawers, or as a curtain or bedspread lining.** **⚠️ And the direct answer to the cost fear: make the coloured items the cheap, replaceable ones.** **⚠️ Limit the palette using nuance combinations — colours adjacent on the wheel — where clashing is unlikely; and match the neutral base's temperature against the accents (cool accents → warmer beige; warm accents → cooler beige or a cool grey).** **Put bolder experiments in transit zones.** If no colour is acceptable: **the achromatic palette is "скорее из области абстракции," because every black and white leans somewhere; use complex colours close to neutral (cream with complex browns, natural leather with ochre, undyed linen); add black to a beige interior for conceptuality, balanced with white; add small details worth examining; and work the textures, because when colour recedes texture comes forward.** **⚠️ The greyscale-photograph test appears here in its sharpest form: the photo must not read as one grey amorphous mush, and the tonal masses must not be equal in scale.** **⚠️ Her verdict, recorded as a claim and a direct challenge to the default: "плохо сделанных нейтральных интерьеров гораздо больше, чем плохо сделанных цветных интерьеров."** [[_Sources/YT_ubB1GSFtN20_kuzina_afraid_of_colour|note]].

- `yt_uRK4OPR4dak` — «Ошибки в декорировании» (2022-09-29, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 24, net of restatement 21), `promotional_ratio: low`. The most-watched video in Cluster 3 by a factor of 1.7.** Structurally better than a list: for each mistake she gives the cause, the fix, **and how to correct it if already made**, several demonstrated as before/after edits she performs on the same image. **⚠️ The sharpest item is a QUALIFICATION OF THIS VAULT'S OWN «поддержка цвета» RULE**: a neutral scheme with yellow elements added fails because *"их оттенок везде одинаковый, и он с такой педантичностью, я бы сказала занудством, повторён"* — **supporting an accent with identical repetitions of itself is a distinct failure from not supporting it at all; the rule is distribute in VARIED shades.** Other load-bearing items: **everything-matched-too-well, with the constraint that the odd piece must still share colour or form**; **proportion — vary adjacent piece sizes, larger pieces often improve a medium room, and "всегда ходите с рулеткой" because a showroom flatters scale**; **"вышибить клин клином"** as the fix for one colour dominating; **declutter BEFORE buying, because the tiredness may be with the quantity ("возможно, усталость как рукой снимет, без дополнительных трат")**; **⚠️ small decor is more dangerous than large, and a collection displayed in full devalues itself — hide part, compose a group, and ROTATE**; **art centred on the owners' eye level, "не Дяди Стёпы"**, never aligned to the door head; **and ⚠️ do not decorate around a purchase you already know is wrong — "в конечном итоге в комнате вас будет раздражать буквально всё," and "сейчас не так сложно продавать мебель, и не обязательно делать это за полцены,"** which supplies the exit the vault's existing sunk-cost material lacked. [[_Sources/YT_uRK4OPR4dak_kuzina_decorating_mistakes|note]].

- `yt_Jj96GbK_35w` — «Как сделать интерьер визуально дороже» (2022-11-24, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 20, net 17). ⚠️ `promotional_ratio: medium` and structurally embedded — her three-step answer terminates in "пригласите специалиста" and she then names her own paid consultation.** A rebuttal of a whole YouTube genre she counted a dozen videos in. **⚠️ Central claim: "ответ очень простой — нужно вложить больше денег," and she audits the standard advice as a list of spends** (engineered-stone sills, taller doors, tubular radiators, more lighting scenarios), with **"есть люди, которые покупают Жигули и потом платят за тюнинг — но от этого девятка не становится Мерседесом."** **⚠️ Her attack on the large-format-art advice is a market-realism argument: the people paying real money for art are not fools who have not heard of Avito; finding suitable large work means knowing which galleries and their prices — and "крупноформатная масляная живопись не будет стоить 10.000 руб (~$140)."** **⚠️ CARRIES THE SECOND DESIGN-FEE BENCHMARK IN THIS VAULT, on a different basis from the first: 10–15% of the project total, with the threshold that if 10% is 20,000 RUB (~$290) you should buy a consultation instead — implying a ~200,000 RUB (~$2,900) total. Cross-checked against Round 3's Moscow absolute floor of 200,000 RUB for a full project: the two are consistent and together bracket where a full engagement becomes purchasable.** **⚠️ And the most transferable item, which is a precondition on every cheap-improvement list this vault holds: "это как у врача — какое лекарство назначать, смотря в каком состоянии пациент." Decorator tricks do nothing on a bad base and genuinely work on a solid one; the genre never states the condition.** Plus the FOURTH statement of her neutral-is-not-a-panacea position, with a new symmetry argument. [[_Sources/YT_Jj96GbK_35w_kuzina_expensive_look_rebuttal|note]].

- `yt_UnCjxyDtWG0` — «Лайфхаки по ремонту из TikTok» (2022-01-26, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 25, net 24), `promotional_ratio: none`. ⚠️ Cluster 1-grade engineering content wearing a methodology title, and the most technically substantial source in Cluster 3.** **⚠️ Her thesis is an epistemic one and it is the real deliverable: the dangerous short-form content is not the wrong content — it is content correct as far as it goes, "вот такой кусок от вот такого объёма," so the viewer finishes with a false sense of understanding while a large layer stayed overboard.** Four of the six lifehacks are true statements that omit a prohibition, a timing window, an installation constraint or a running cost. **⚠️ GAS SAFETY, which this vault had no coverage of at all: enclosing a gas water heater in a cabinet is prohibited, as is hiding gas pipes behind panels, in cabinets or chased into walls — "пожар, взрыв газа, или как минимум долгие обсуждения с газовой службой."** **⚠️ THE LEAK-SENSOR TIMING ARGUMENT, read off Gidrolock's own published site on camera and the single most consequential item in Round 5: detection takes 2 seconds, but the valve then takes a further 15–20 seconds to close (up to 40 with other suppliers) — so a threshold contains the water released during that window while its absence lets it spread through the flat. An engineering argument for a threshold that survives outside Russia, unlike the regulatory one.** Plus: **the Russian threshold norm and its replanning-approval consequence, flagged as Russian and routed to the bathroom page, NOT to `16_Legal_and_Regulations/`**; **the mini-ramp-in-the-opening alternative with the door-lining detail a fitter will otherwise cut straight**; **⚠️ underfloor-heating cable is never cut on site, and being able to run it up a wall means you over-ordered — "это просто результат их собственного просчёта," plus the running cost of towel and floor on one circuit**; painting tile as a legitimate cheap refresh with the peeling caveat; and plain tap water removing old wallpaper as well as anything. [[_Sources/YT_UnCjxyDtWG0_kuzina_tiktok_lifehacks_debunked|note]].

- `yt_JOBm37_9iDg` — «Практичный интерьер» (2020-10-05, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 35, net 31) — the highest yield in Round 5 and the most useful source in it for a live renovation, because it is organised around the decision every line item actually faces.** `promotional_ratio: none`, unusual for 28 minutes. **⚠️ Her definition is worth adopting: "часто основной вопрос практичности — это то, к чему приводит взаимодействие человека с материалом" — not a property of the object but of the contact.** **⚠️ THE THESIS: "практичный интерьер — не синоним хорошего интерьера," carried by a paired comparison table across eight material decisions, and closing on where pure practicality leads — "обычно практично всё то, что выглядит скучно," arriving at "интерьер в стиле Ашан" on a small budget or "интерьер в стиле павильонов Мис ван дер Роэ" on a large one.** **⚠️ THE HIGHEST-CONSEQUENCE ITEM, and the vault had nothing on it: a miscalculated FINISHED-FLOOR LEVEL makes an interior unusable — her example is a WC set so high the feet do not reach, and "ошибка там в 3–4 сантиметра реально делает интерьер адски неудобным." The fault is arithmetic at rough stage, not fitting at fit-out.** Other load-bearing items: **⚠️ fingerprints are decided by COATING, not colour — two Geberit Sigma plates, the BLACK one with an anti-fingerprint coating showing nothing and the LIGHT one showing far more**; **the acrylic-versus-quartz worktop comparison on restorability, deliberately left open**; **coloured-through versus surface-coloured for porcelain, sanitaryware and parquet**; hardness and ageing (chipboard vs MDF, the timber ordering); **⚠️ a third category — materials that age beautifully, and styles (loft, Provence, boho) that presume imperfection and so get ageing free**; internal corners not at 90°; **a 2× multiplier for premium kitchen internals (Blum named)**; **a dissent on dimmers as an interface**; **a THIRD distinct objection to cove lighting (the channel is unreachable and never cleaned)**; **the acceptance point — a beautifully designed restaurant washroom whose latch would not engage**; and an honest section on **cases where it is genuinely unclear which option is more practical.** [[_Sources/YT_JOBm37_9iDg_kuzina_practical_vs_good_interior|note]].

- `yt_YaIuW1lX1sU` — «Фокусная точка» (2024-03-12, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 30, net 29) — the least redundant source in Round 5, because the organising concept is new to this vault.** `promotional_ratio: low`, **and as the only 2024 upload processed it is a useful midpoint for the channel-format observation: a light closing pitch and NO Telegram funnel, unlike the two 2026 uploads.** **⚠️ The problem it names: "бывает, что пространство выглядит хаотичным и неорганизованным даже при том, что там хорошие формы мебели и удачная цветовая гамма — дело в фокусной точке, вернее в её отсутствии."** **⚠️ The definition and the constraint that follows: a focal point is set by the designer OR by the architect (bay window, niche, projection, window, staircase) — and where the architecture already sets one, the designer's job is to SUPPORT it, not contradict it.** **⚠️ Placement rests on one geometric fact: we look most easily at what is parallel to our face, so a focal point usually carries a vertical component — but it can sit on the floor (entrance groups) or the ceiling (sleeping zones).** **⚠️ Two approaches, and the better one is the delicate version where the focal point sets the style and everything else supports it — her worked example has a chandelier echoing the wreath in a photograph, chair upholstery echoing the dress edging, and ceiling moulding echoing the implied classicism. "Support" is not colour-matching; it is repeating forms and references.** **⚠️ THE ONE DIMENSION: art above a sofa must be ≥ half the sofa's length, better ⅔ — and if it is not, make up the distance with sconces or side lamps.** Plus: an accent wall is not always self-sufficient and wants a focal point against it; highlighting a large object makes it read larger (keep the wardrobe neutral and brighten its interior instead); a non-vertical object needs accessories or fittings above to become a readable GROUP; the bedroom split (large room accent the whole bed, small room accent the headboard and strip the horizontal surface); **and the multi-focus rules — one per zone, agreeing in style and meaning, never two side by side in one plane, "глазам нужна пауза, а фокусная точка должна дышать," and one must dominate while the other supports.** **A dedicated page was warranted on content but withheld as `single-account` — logged in `Pending_Wiki_Page_Decisions.md`.** [[_Sources/YT_YaIuW1lX1sU_kuzina_focal_point|note]].

- `yt_NenrhBgNxGI` — «Как улучшить любой интерьер» (2022-08-04, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 16, ⚠️ net of restatement only 11 — the lowest net yield and the lowest ratio (69%) of any source across five rounds).** `promotional_ratio: low`. **⚠️ This is precisely the failure mode Round 5's dispatch note predicted for Cluster 3: not a low fact count, but facts new to the page they land on and not new to the vault.** Five items substantially restate held material — the verb-list functionality questions, the minimum-three-light-sources rule, the art-selection parameters, and the mix-don't-match rule. **Recorded plainly rather than counted as coverage; two of the restated items were deliberately NOT routed and that decision is noted in the extraction note so a later reader does not assume they were missed.** **Its genuine value is concentrated in four items**: **⚠️ the rug rules — no rug is acceptable in a small bedroom but leaves a living room incomplete, refusing one means devising other floor zoning "и это всегда более сложное в исполнении дизайна," too-small rugs spoil proportion, and "правильный размер ковра имеет больше значения для дизайна, чем его цвет и рисунок"**; **⚠️ the glass test — "рядом с любым местом для сидения есть поверхность, куда можно поставить стакан," with the bedroom variant of a surface behind the headboard where no nightstand fits**; **⚠️ the something-living observation, which explains a convention rather than repeating it — published interiors always contain plants because plants remove the feeling of abandonment on a shoot and behave better on camera than pets, "но они так же хорошо выглядят в интерьере просто, не на фото"**; and **something old — "следы времени вселяют душу в новый интерьер."** Plus gloss on matte walls, and the conditional that a poster or photograph needs handmade objects around it in a way an original does not. [[_Sources/YT_NenrhBgNxGI_kuzina_improving_any_interior|note]].

- `yt_vnLZqiYxY1Q` — «Почему дорогие интерьеры цветные, а бюджетные бежевые» (2026-06-25, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 19, net 17). ⚠️ `promotional_ratio: medium` — the second consecutive 2026 upload with a Telegram funnel, which CONFIRMS the Round 4 channel-format observation as a pattern rather than a one-off.** **⚠️ The most structurally-argued source from this channel: it explains a design outcome by the economics of how the products are sold. "Хорошая палитра в интерьере — часто вопрос не вкуса, а бюджета и доступа к индивидуальным решениям."** **⚠️ AND IT CONTAINS THE BEST STEELMAN OF THE NEUTRAL DEFAULT ANYWHERE IN THIS VAULT, from the practitioner who has attacked it four times: someone assembling an interior themselves is primarily REDUCING RISK — "нейтральная палитра кажется безопасной именно потому, что её сложнее испортить; она легче собирается по частям и меньше зависит от идеального совпадения оттенков." This identifies the variable the whole dispute turns on and makes it resolvable: neutral is the right answer for an interior assembled INCREMENTALLY BY A NON-EXPERT, which is a different question from whether it is right aesthetically.** Other items: **white kitchen fronts at roughly 40–45% of mass-market choice for years** (research house not recoverable from the ASR); **bright colour exists on the shop floor to stop the gaze — the attention economy, with a cited but unrecoverable claim that up to 90% of first impression is colour**; **⚠️ the structural reason complex colour is not sold in shopping centres — "её продавать неудобно": hard to show quickly, hard to explain if the salesperson is not a colour expert, and it requires accounting for colours of products the company does not sell**; **⚠️ the missing middle — no warm smoky greys, olives, garnets or wines, because complex shades produce no instant effect and "не раскрываются в маленьком образце," so on a stand they look dirty**; the Josef Albers attribution for the relativity principle the vault held unsourced; **⚠️ and the punchline figure: "нестандартный цвет эмали увеличивает стоимость фасада на 10, максимум 20%" — so "иногда разница начинается с того, что один человек выбирал между шестью готовыми фасадами, а другой — между сотней сложных оттенков." A choice-set gap before it is a budget gap.** [[_Sources/YT_vnLZqiYxY1Q_kuzina_mass_market_palette_economics|note]].

- `yt_HENjGv0DGjI` — «Колор блок» (2022-05-19, Надежда Кузина) — **FULL (fact_yield 26, net 25), `promotional_ratio: none`. Promoted out of Round 4's deferral list, where it had been set aside as a likely overlap with `lptrnXyf0as`; the overlap does not exist beyond one cross-reference she makes herself.** **⚠️ She rejects the standard definition (combining complementary colours) as arbitrary and supplies her own, all three parameters required: large colour patches; contrast BY COLOUR (the simplest of Itten's seven); and "плоскость важнее объёма."** **⚠️ AND IT CONTAINS A SCOPED REVERSAL OF A RULE THIS VAULT HOLDS: in colour block, "лучше использовать три ярких пятна, три цвета, чем восемь цветов в мелких элементах," and elements of one colour are grouped TOGETHER — "здесь нет задачи размазать или как-то распределить цвета по интерьеру." Colour support DISTRIBUTES an accent; colour block CONSOLIDATES it. Different techniques with opposite rules, recorded as such rather than filed alongside.** **⚠️ The operational test that distinguishes it from merely bright walls: two interiors with bright emerald walls, one lacking colour contrast (green plus bright metal) and therefore just bright plain walls; the other with green walls, YELLOW floor and blue chairs, reading flatter — that is colour block.** Plus: **the three weaknesses — it takes all the attention so becomes the only technique the interior rests on, it leaves no place for small detail, and it often fragments a room**; **⚠️ a staged adoption path (transit zones → bathrooms → bedrooms) with an explicit warning that small bright objects do NOT predict how you will feel about large ones**; coloured outlines as the low-commitment version; **⚠️ the four cases where flat painted shapes are justified, and the failure where painted rectangles meet mouldings — "стереоскопическая модель: один глаз видит молдинги, другой — цветовые пятна"**; primaries giving the strongest contrast, declining off the wheel's centre; and **the gradient at which colour block becomes wall painting — recognisable forms end it.** **A substantial De Stijl/Bauhaus lineage argument (that it reached interiors via Mondrian → Bauhaus rather than via Saint Laurent) was deliberately NOT routed as art history, per this vault's standing deprioritisation; only Alvar Aalto's 1929 hospital was kept, as the one case tying the technique to a stated purpose.** [[_Sources/YT_HENjGv0DGjI_kuzina_colour_block|note]].

### Татьяна Михайловская — a new channel, 3 of 26 videos taken (2026-09-01)

**⚠️ Channel context, because it decides how to read these three.** The playlist «Курс дизайн интерьера» is **26 videos / 29 h 55 min, of which 22 (~27 h) were REJECTED at triage** — lessons 1–19 are a vocational 3ds Max / Revit / Corona course for aspiring visualisers, confirmed by sampling three lessons at eight points. Full reasoning in `_Inbox/planning/mikhailovskaya_course_triage_20260901.md`. **The three taken are standalone videos appended to the course, and are a different kind of content entirely.**

**⚠️ And the discriminator that made the triage possible is worth carrying forward: on this channel it is not FORMAT but AUDIENCE. Material addressed to designers learning a trade is useless; material addressed to a client making a decision is useful.** These three are the exception within designer-facing material, because **a walkthrough of what a designer DELIVERS is simultaneously a checklist of what a client should DEMAND.**

- `yt_MkssMwpyVsI` — «Состав дизайн-проекта» (2023-07-10, Татьяна Михайловская) — **FULL (fact_yield 27, net 25). ⚠️ The ONLY manually-captioned source in this vault (`generated=False`)** — transcript quality far above any auto-generated Russian source processed here. `promotional_ratio: low`, **and the criterion matters: she directs viewers to her own Revit and 3ds Max tutorials, but NOTHING IS WITHHELD to that funnel — the full sheet list and reasoning are given.** *(A pitch that FOLLOWS complete content is `low`; a pitch that REPLACES content is `medium`, per the Кузина 2026 precedent.)* **⚠️ A SECOND INDEPENDENT CHANNEL on designer scope of service, and it supplies what Кузина's `H43m5bn7W8k` lacked: that source gives the STAGES of a design project, this gives the DELIVERABLES.** **Three tiers**: **эскизный** (visualisation explicitly without reference to reality — appropriate when either side is unsure, or when the client needs only the idea, *"не много таких случаев"*); **стандартный пакет** (the minimum that can be built from — real materials, plus obligatory specifications and counts of fittings and switches); **полный** (plus additional drawings and **3D views of contentious junctions — furniture-to-wall, a special panel — drawn "чтобы не было допущено мебельщиками или строителями каких-то ошибок," i.e. a defect-prevention device for the trades, not a sales image**). **Then a 19-sheet list**, title page through specifications. **⚠️ Five items a client would not know to ask for, each with a stated reason**: **tile setting-out is the designer's obligation and belongs on BOTH the floor plan and the elevations, because the tiler works from the elevations**; **underfloor heating needs zone AREAS stated for purchasing, wall offsets drawn, nothing under furniture or appliances, and client agreement**; **⚠️ SEPARATE BLACK-AND-WHITE ELECTRICAL ELEVATIONS — she says she has not seen others do it — because "многие розетки находятся за мебелью, электровывод можно упустить, который в потолке"**; **⚠️ LABEL EVERY OUTLET including ones no switch controls, because an unlabelled outlet is ambiguous between "deliberately unswitched" and "you forgot" — "для того чтобы вы могли сами себя перепроверить"**; and **elevations should label the MECHANISM** (which drawer is push-to-open, where a handle is, lift-up opening). **⚠️ Also a detail that lands on Кузина Round 5's finished-floor finding: her project goes 2,800 → 2,650 mm after floor levelling, carried explicitly from survey plan to post-replanning plan — the documentation control for the error that source identified.** [[_Sources/YT_MkssMwpyVsI_mikhailovskaya_design_project_composition|note]].

- `yt_Lqx2GSllqFQ` — «Ванная. Планировка и сантехника. Часть 1» (2023-08-07, Татьяна Михайловская) — **FULL (fact_yield 42, net 38), `promotional_ratio: none` across 61 minutes. ⚠️ The strongest DIMENSIONAL source routed to this vault's bathroom pages**, because she plans **three real bathrooms live on screen** — placing blocks, measuring gaps and **rejecting arrangements when the numbers fail** — so the dimensions are constraints that killed specific layouts rather than a table of minimums. **Method**: **locate the sewer riser first — "самое важное — это никуда не переместить унитаз"**; **rough blocks before any detail**; start from the BATH where there is one; **and a bathroom needing replanning is not a local decision — "планировка должна разрабатываться в комплексе."** **⚠️ Working dimensions**: vanity depth **550–600** (she uses 600); built-in washing machine **600**, free-standing **650–680** because of clearances and servicing; **shower entry 600 minimum — at 660 she only just passes, 540 is impossible**; boiler **~400 × 500**; **shower enclosure 900 × 900 minimum**; **bath 1600 minimum to bathe adequately**; partition **~50 + tile ≈ 100**. **⚠️ The setting-out trap that killed a layout: count the adhesive AND the tile when sizing a partition — she needed 650 clear and had 601.** **⚠️ Constraints**: **a boiler cannot hang on a non-load-bearing wall without a welded steel frame, which must be in the project**; a glass panel enclosing an unreachable gap collects dust and condensate and cannot be wiped; **a stationary tiled partition, not glass, between a shower and free-standing machines** — though **glass reads semi-transparent and so eats less space than a solid unit**. **⚠️ Bath materials: steel cheapest, acrylic mid, cast iron dearest — and acrylic is her universal recommendation.** **⚠️ MIXERS, the strongest section: "длина излива — это самый главный параметр подбора ванны и смесителя"** — the spout must project beyond the rim or water pours onto it; **a rim mixer fits acrylic without difficulty and may not fit other bath materials, so the mixer choice effectively decides the bath material**; **check the mixer's widest part against the rim and phone the supplier, because drawings often omit it**; floor-standing mixers are decorative and wrong for *"90 процентов случаев"*; **specify a spout even on a shower-only mixer, "по крайней мере набрать ванную для ребёнка"**; round over square for maintenance. **⚠️ Flush floor-level showers: "красиво на картинке, но максимально непрактичные в жизни" — minimum 2 cm fall, better to sink the tray, and in a flat make a threshold. A SECOND INDEPENDENT ARGUMENT for a threshold, from drainage and serviceability where Кузина's comes from leak-sensor shutoff timing.** **Trap selection by capacity AND width.** **⚠️ And a programme decision: "душевая кабина — она не ванная" — fit even a small sit-bath if possible, because "актуальность ванны резко вырастет с появлением детей," and warning the client is framed as an obligation.** [[_Sources/YT_Lqx2GSllqFQ_mikhailovskaya_bathroom_planning_bath_shower|note]].

- `yt_yqb7N1yA9hg` — «Ванная. Часть 2: унитаз, раковина, смесители» (2023-08-15, Татьяна Михайловская) — **FULL (fact_yield 45, net 42) — the highest single-source yield in this vault.** `promotional_ratio: low` (closing subscribe request only). **⚠️ The stronger half of the pair, because it carries the COUPLINGS that decide a layout — chains where choosing one component silently forecloses another.** **⚠️ THE BEST ITEM, and it follows arithmetically rather than being asserted: a vessel basin and an under-worktop washing machine are MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE. The standard is 850 mm from finished floor to the basin's top edge; a vessel bowl ~100 high puts the worktop at ~750; a washing machine is 860–870 high and will not go under it. "Если в планировке заложено расположение стиральной машины под столешницей, тогда раковина-чаша вам не подходит" — it would put the rim at 950–960. So the basin TYPE is dictated by the plan, not by taste.** **⚠️ Basin type also decides the worktop material (an undermount basin is GLUED, so no chipboard and better no timber) AND the drawer layout (with an inset basin the bowl and trap occupy the cabinet, so a full-width drawer beneath CANNOT EXIST — she demonstrates this on a real rendering).** **⚠️ Basin mixers: spout length again, worked — a shallow basin (350) set 150 off the wall against a 100 spout pours water BEHIND the basin; ≤20 mm from basin edge to worktop edge, ideally flush; some basins arrive with a tap hole and so cannot take a wall mixer; therefore "выбрали раковину, а потом может быть когда-то выберу смеситель — нет, так не работает."** **⚠️ WC and frame: "унитаз не терпит прямых углов" in the drain run;
